the set of all formal Taylor power series

a(z)=a_{1}z+a_{2}z^{2}+a_{3}z^{3}+\dotsb
with a vanishing coefficient
in degree
0
and a nonvanishing coefficient
a_{1}\neq 0
in degree
1
.
Then the composition multiplication
(a*b)(z)=a(b(z))
defines
a group structure on the set
H(k)
.
This group is naturally the group of
k
-points of a certain
pro-affine pro-algebraic group, which we denote by
H
.
The Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{h}}
of the pro-algebraic group
H
can be easily
identified with the algebra of vector fields on the formal disk
with a vanishing vector at the origin
zk[[z]]d/dz
, or with
the closed subalgebra in the Virasoro Lie algebra topologically
spanned by the basis vectors
L_{0}
,
L_{1}
,
L_{2}
, …
(see Section
1.7
).

Let us say a few words about Lie theory in the pro-algebraic
group setting.
For any subcoalgebra
{\mathcal{D}}
in a commutative Hopf algebra
{\mathcal{C}}
,
the subalgebra generated by
{\mathcal{D}}+s({\mathcal{D}})
in a Hopf subalgebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
.
Since
{\mathcal{C}}
is the union of its finite-dimensional subcoalgebras
(see Section
1.3
), it is also the union of
its Hopf subalgebras that are finitely generated as commutative
algebras.
Thus there is no difference between the notions of a pro-affine
pro-algebraic variety with a group structure and a pro-object in
the category of affine algebraic groups.
The Lie functor on the category of (pro-afffine) pro-algebraic
groups can be simply obtained by passing to the pro-objects on
both sides of the functor assigning a Lie algebra to
an algebraic group; so the Lie algebra of a pro-algebraic group is
a filtered projective limit of finite-dimensional Lie algebras.
In particular, it follows that the topological Lie algebra

k[[z]]d/dz
, which has no closed ideals (see Section
2.4
),

cannot
correspond to any pro-algebraic group, though its
subalgebra
zk[[z]]d/dz
does, as we have just seen.

We are interested in considering Harish-Chandra pairs with
a Tate Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
and a pro-algebraic subgroup
H
corresponding
to an open linearly compact subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
.
A precise definition presents a small technical difficulty in that
one has to explain what it means for the coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}(H)
to act
in a Tate vector space
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
Neither the notion of a
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
nor
that of
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule are suitable for the task; rather, the compact
vector space
{\mathfrak{h}}
, being dual to a
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule, can be viewed as
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule, while the quotient space
{\mathfrak{g}}/{\mathfrak{h}}
has
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule structure.

However, an action of a algebraic group in a vector space is,
of course,
not
determined by its restriction to an invariant
subspace and the induced action in the quotient space.
The authors of the manuscript
[
13
]
, where (what we call)
Tate Harish-Chandra pairs seem to have first appeared, solve
the problem by working over the field of complex numbers
\mathbb{C}

and considering an action of the
group of points

H(\mathbb{C})
of a pro-algebraic group
H
in a Tate Lie
algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}

[
13
, Section 3.1]
.
The approach taken in
[
67
, Appendix D]
, which works over
an arbitrary field, is to give the definition of a
continuous
coaction
of a discrete coalgebra in a topological vector space.

Here we restrict ourselves to a brief sketch.
Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a coassociative coalgebra and
V
be a topological
vector space over
k
(see
Sections
2.3
–
2.4
).
A
continuous right coaction
of
{\mathcal{C}}
in
V
is a continuous
linear map
V\longrightarrow V\otimes^{!}{\mathcal{C}}
, where
{\mathcal{C}}
is considered as
a discrete topological vector space, satisfying the coassociativity
and counitality equations.
Equivalently, a continuous right coaction can be defined as
a continuous linear map
V\otimes^{*}{\mathcal{C}}^{*}\longrightarrow V
, where
{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
is
viewed as a linearly compact vector space, satisfying
the associativity and unitality equations.

For any topological vector space
V
with a continuous coaction of
a coassociative coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
, open subspaces of
V
invariant
under the continuous coaction form a base of neighborhoods of zero
in
V
.
Given a topological vector space
V
endowed with a continuous
coaction of a coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
and a topological vector space
W

endowed with a continuous coaction of a coalgebra
{\mathcal{D}}
, all
the three topological tensor products
V\otimes^{!}W
,
V\otimes^{*}W
, and

V\mathbin{\overset{\leftarrow}{\vphantom{o}\smash{\otimes}}}W
are naturally endowed with a continuous coaction of
the coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{D}}
.
In particular, when two topological vector spaces
V
and
W
are
endowed with continuous coactions of a Hopf algebra
{\mathcal{C}}
,
the three topological tensor products acquire the tensor product
coactions of
{\mathcal{C}}

[
67
, Sections D.1.3–4]
.

A continuous coaction of a commutative Hopf algebra
{\mathcal{C}}
in
a topological Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
is said to be
compatible
with
the Lie algebra structure if the bracket map
[\,,\,]\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes^{*}{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
commutes with the continuous coactions of
{\mathcal{C}}
.
Similarly one can speak about compatibility of topological
associative algebra structures, continuous actions, pairings,
etc. with continuous coactions of
{\mathcal{C}}

[
67
, Section D.1.5]
.
A Tate Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
with a continuous coaction of
a commutative Hopf algebra
{\mathcal{C}}
compatible with the Lie algebra
structure has a base of neighborhoods of zero consisting of

{\mathcal{C}}
-invariant compact open Lie subalgebras
{\mathfrak{h}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}

[
67
, Section D.1.8]
.

A
Tate Harish-Chandra pair

({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
is a set of data consisting
of a Tate Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
, a commutative Hopf algebra
{\mathcal{C}}
,
a continuous coaction of
{\mathcal{C}}
in
{\mathfrak{g}}
compatible with the Lie algebra
structure, a
{\mathcal{C}}
-invariant compact open subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
,
and a continuous pairing
\psi\colon{\mathcal{C}}\times{\mathfrak{h}}\longrightarrow k
, where
{\mathcal{C}}
is
endowed with the discrete topology.
These data should satisfy four compatibility equations:
the pairing
\psi
should be compatible with the comultiplication
in
{\mathcal{C}}
and the Lie bracket in
{\mathfrak{g}}
, and also with the multiplication
in
{\mathcal{C}}
; the pairing
\psi
should be compatible with the restriction
to
{\mathfrak{h}}
of the continuous coaction of
{\mathcal{C}}
in
{\mathfrak{g}}
and the adjoint
coaction of
{\mathcal{C}}
in itself; the action of
{\mathfrak{h}}
in
{\mathfrak{g}}
obtained by
deriving the continuous coaction of
{\mathcal{C}}
in
{\mathfrak{g}}
using
the paring
\psi
should coincide with the adjoint action of
{\mathfrak{h}}

in
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
We refer to
[
67
, Section D.2.1]
for the details.

In particular, let
{\mathfrak{g}}
be a Tate Lie algebra with a pro-nilpotent
compact open subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
, i. e.,
{\mathfrak{h}}
is the projective
limit of a filtered projective system of finite-dimensional
nilpotent Lie algebras.
Assume further that the discrete
{\mathfrak{h}}
-module
{\mathfrak{g}}/{\mathfrak{h}}
is nilpotent
or “ind-nilpotent”, i. e., it is the union of finite-dimensional
nilpotent modules over finite-dimensional quotient Lie algebras
of
{\mathfrak{h}}
by its open ideals.
Then, over a field of characteristic
0
, one can integrate
the pair of Lie algebras
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathfrak{h}})
to a Tate Harish-Chandra pair
with the Hopf algebra
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}(H)
of the pro-unipotent pro-algebraic
group
H
corresponding to the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{h}}
.
A version of this construction is applicable over fields
of arbitrary characteristic
[
67
, Section D.6]
.
In particular, let
{\mathfrak{g}}=\bigoplus_{n<0}{\mathfrak{g}}_{n}\oplus\prod_{n\geqslant 0}{\mathfrak{g}}_{n}

be the Laurent totalization a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded Lie algebra with
finite-dimensional components (see Section
2.4
); then
for any integer
m\geqslant 1
the Lie subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}=\prod_{n\geqslant m}{\mathfrak{g}}_{m}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
satisfies the above nilpotency conditions, so there is
the corresponding Tate Harish-Chandra pair
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
.

A
Harish-Chandra module

{\mathcal{M}}
over a Tate Harish-Chandra pair

({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
is a
k
-vector space endowed with a discrete
{\mathfrak{g}}
-module
and a
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule structures satisfying the following two
compatibility equations.
Firstly, the derivative
{\mathfrak{h}}
-action of the
{\mathcal{C}}
-coaction in
{\mathcal{M}}
,
which is always a discrete action due to the continuity/discreteness
condition imposed on the pairing
\psi
, should coincide with
the restriction of the
{\mathfrak{g}}
-action in
{\mathcal{M}}
to
{\mathfrak{h}}
.
Secondly, the action map
{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes^{*}{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}
should be compatible
with the continuous coactions of
{\mathcal{C}}
; equivalently, the action map

{\mathfrak{g}}/U\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}
should be a morphism of
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules for
any finite-dimensional
{\mathcal{C}}
-subcomodule
{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}
and any

{\mathcal{C}}
-invariant compact open subspace
U\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
annihilating
{\mathcal{L}}

[
67
, Section D.2.5]
.

The pairing
\psi\colon{\mathcal{C}}\times{\mathfrak{h}}\longrightarrow k
can be viewed as a Lie
algebra morphism
{\mathfrak{h}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
(where the Lie algebra structure
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
is defined in terms of its associative algebra structure),
and as such, can be uniquely extended to an associative algebra
morphism
U({\mathfrak{h}})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
, providing a pairing
\phi\colon{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}U({\mathfrak{h}})\longrightarrow k
compatible with the Hopf algebra structures on both sides.
When the pairing
\phi
is nondegenerate in
{\mathcal{C}}

[
67
, condition D.2.2 (iv)]
, the derivative action
functor
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{h}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--discr}}}
is fully
faithful
[
67
, Section 10.1.4]
, which allows to simplify
the definition of a Harish-Chandra module over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
.
Namely, the second one of the above two compatibility equations
holds automatically in this case and can be dropped, so
Harish-Chandra modules over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
can be simply defined as
discrete
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules whose discrete
{\mathfrak{h}}
-module structure
comes from a
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule structure (cf.
[
67
, Section D.2.2]
).
In particular, this nondegeneracy condition holds in the above
example of a Tate Harish-Chandra pair associated with a Tate
Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
with a pro-nilpotent compact open subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}

acting ind-nilpotently in
{\mathfrak{g}}/{\mathfrak{h}}
(over any field
k
).

A
Harish-Chandra contramodule

{\mathfrak{P}}
over a Tate Harish-Chandra
pair
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
is a
k
-vector space endowed with a
{\mathfrak{g}}
-contramodule
and a
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule structures satisfying the following two
compatibility equations.
Firstly, the contraderivative
{\mathfrak{h}}
-contraaction of
the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contraaction in
{\mathfrak{P}}
, which is defined as the composition

{\mathfrak{h}}\otimes^{^}\,{\mathfrak{P}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\simeq\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Hom_{k}({\mathfrak{h}}^{\vee},{\mathfrak{P}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathfrak{P}}

of the map induced by the pairing
\psi
and the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contraaction map,
should coincide with the restriction of the
{\mathfrak{g}}
-contraaction in
{\mathfrak{P}}

to the subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}
(see the definition of a contramodule over
a Tate Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
in Section
2.4
).
Secondly, for any
{\mathcal{C}}
-invariant compact open subspace
U\subset{\mathfrak{g}}

the
{\mathfrak{g}}
-contraaction map
\Hom_{k}(U^{\vee},{\mathfrak{P}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
should be
a morphism of
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules (where the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
structure on the Hom space from a
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
U^{\vee}
into
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
is provided by the construction from
Section
2.7
).
This definition can be found in
[
67
, Sections D.2.7–8]
.

Moreover, under some additional assumptions this definition of
the category of Harish-Chandra contramodules can be also simplified,
similarly to the above discussion of the simpler definition of
the category of Harish-Chandra modules.
See Theorem
3.8
.1, Corollary
3.8
,
and Example
3.8
.2 below.

For any Harish-Chandra module
{\mathcal{M}}
over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
and any
k
-vector
space
E
, the vector space
{\mathfrak{P}}=\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{M}},E)
has a natural
Harish-Chandra contramodule structure with the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contraaction in

{\mathfrak{P}}
provided by the construction from Section
1.2

and the
{\mathfrak{g}}
-contraaction in
{\mathfrak{P}}
defined according to
the construction from Section
2.4
.

The categories
{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
and
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
of Harish-Chandra
modules and contramodules over a Tate Harish-Chandra pair
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

are abelian, and the forgetful functors
{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}

and
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
are exact.
Both the infinite direct sums and infinite products exist in
the cagories
{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
and
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
, but only the direct
sums are preserved by the forgetful functor
{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}

and only the products are preserved by the functor

{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
.
The category
{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
satisfies the axioms Ab5 and Ab3*
(but not Ab4*), while the category
{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
satisfies
the axioms Ab3 and Ab4* (but not Ab4 or Ab5*).

There are enough injective objects in the category
{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

and enough projective objects in the category
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
.
The identification of these categories with categories of
semimodules and semicontramodules over certain semialgebras, which
we will now briefly discuss, will make the explicit constructions
of such injective and projective objects explained below in
Section
3.5
applicable in this case.

As in Section
2.7
, the semialgebras
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
are defined as the tensor products

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}=U({\mathfrak{g}})\otimes_{U({\mathfrak{h}})}{\mathcal{C}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}={\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{U({\mathfrak{h}})}U({\mathfrak{g}}),

where, as above,
U({\mathfrak{g}})
and
U({\mathfrak{h}})
denote the universal enveloping
algebras of the Lie algebras
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
{\mathfrak{h}}
considered as abstract
Lie algebras without any topologies.
The left and right
U({\mathfrak{h}})
-module structures on
{\mathcal{C}}
are obtained
by deriving the left and right coactions of
{\mathcal{C}}
in itself using
the pairing
\phi
.
The right coaction of
{\mathcal{C}}
in
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}
and the left coaction in
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}

are induced by the right and left coactions of
{\mathcal{C}}
in itself.
The construction of the left coaction of
{\mathcal{C}}
in
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}
and the right
coaction in
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}
is rather delicate
[
67
, Section D.2.3]
.
Once the
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{C}}
-bicomodule structures on
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}

has been defined, the constructions of the semimultiplication and
semiunit maps are similar to those in
Section
2.7
(see
[
67
, Section 10.2.1]
),
though one still has to check that the maps
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}
so obtained are morphisms of

{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{C}}
-bicomodules (the nontrivial part is to show that
the former is a morphism of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules and
the latter a morphism of right ones).

Now the category
{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
of Harish-Chandra modules over
a Tate Harish-Chandra pair
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
is isomorphic to the category
of left semimodules over the semialgebra
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
; the datum
of a left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}
-semimodule structure on a
k
-vector space
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}

is equivalent to that of a Harish-Chandra module structure on
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
.
Similarly, the category
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
of Harish-Chandra
contramodules over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
is isomorphic to the category of
left semicontramodules over the semialgebra
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
; the datum of
a left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}
-semicontramodule structure on a
k
-vector space
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}

is equivalent to that a Harish-Chandra contramodule structure on
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}

[
67
, Sections 10.2.2, D.2.5, and D.2.8]
.

As in Section
2.7
, one would like to have also
an explicit description of what it means to have a left

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}
-semimodule structure on a
k
-vector space
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
.
In the infinite-dimensional situation, one cannot hope to have
a Morita equivalence between the semialgebras
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}
.
Rather, the determinantal anomaly moves one step higher in
the cohomological degree when one passes to Tate vector spaces,
and what used to be a twist with the modular character in
the finite-dimensional case becomes
a shift of the central
charge
in the Tate Harish-Chandra situation.
Before formulating the precise assertion, let us give the definition
of a
central extension

\varkappa\colon({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

of a Tate Harish-Chandra pair
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
.

A
morphism of Tate Harish-Chandra pairs

({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})\longrightarrow({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
can be defined as a set of data consisting of
a continuous morphism of Tate Lie algebras
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
and
a morphism of Hopf algebras
{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
such that the map

{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
commutes with the continuous
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
-coactions and
takes the subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
into the subalgebra

{\mathfrak{h}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
, while the maps
{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{h}}

are compatible with the pairings
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
.
A
central extension of Tate Harish-Chandra pairs with
the kernel
k
is a morphism
({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})\longrightarrow({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

such that
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{C}}
is the same Hopf algebra and
{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}

the identity map,
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
is a quotient map of topological
vector spaces with a one-dimensional kernel
k\subset{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
in
which a fixed basis vector
1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}\in k\subset{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
is chosen,
the map of Lie subalgebras
{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{h}}
is an isomorphism,
the kernel
k=k\cdot 1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
lies in the center of
the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
, and the coaction of the Hopf algebra
{\mathcal{C}}

in the subspace
k\subset{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
is trivial
[
67
, Section D.2.2]
.
As it is usual for extensions with a fixed kernel, the set of
all (isomorphism classes of) central extensions of a given Tate
Harish-Chandra pair
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
with the kernel
k
has a natural
structure of abelian group (and in fact, even of a
k
-vector
space) with respect to the Baer sum
operation
[
67
, Section D.3.1]
.

The Lie algebra
\gl(V)
of continuous endomorphisms of a Tate
vector space
V
has a canonical central extension
\gl(V)^{~}

with the kernel
k
defined in terms of the trace functional
on the space of all continuous linear operators
V\longrightarrow V

of finite rank
[
14
, Sections 2.7.8 and 3.8.17–18]

(see also
[
67
, Sections D.1.6–8]
; a historical discussion
can be found in
[
14
, the beginning of Section 2.7]
).
The central extension
\gamma_{0}\colon\gl(V)^{~}\longrightarrow\gl(V)
can be
characterized by the property that there is a well-defined linear action
of the Lie algebra
\gl(V)^{~}
in the space
\bigwedge^{\infty/2}(V)
of
semi-infinite exterior forms over
V
lifting the projective action
of the Lie algebra
\gl(V)
(see
[
39
]
,
[
51
, Lecture 4]
,
and
[
15
, Sections 4.2.13 and 7.13.16]
).
One chooses the canonical basis element
1_{\gl^{~}}\in k\subset\gl(V)^{~}
so that it acts by the identity operator in
the space of semi-infinite forms; abusing the terminology, we say
that
\bigwedge^{\infty/2}(V)
is a “
\gl(V)
-module with
the central charge
\gamma_{0}
”.

Pulling back the central extension
\gl({\mathfrak{g}})^{~}\longrightarrow\gl({\mathfrak{g}})
with
respect to the adjoint representation
\ad_{\mathfrak{g}}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\gl({\mathfrak{g}})
, one
obtains the canonical central extension
\varkappa_{0}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
of
an arbitrary Tate Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
The above convention for the choice of the canonical basis element

1_{\gl^{~}}
and the consequent choice of the basis element

1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}}\in k\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
provide it that for any discrete module
M

over the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}
where the element
1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}}

acts by minus the identity operator (“a discrete
{\mathfrak{g}}
-module with
the central charge
-\varkappa_{0}
”) there is a well-defined discrete
action of the original Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
in the tensor product

\bigwedge^{\infty/2}(V)\otimes_{k}M
, allowing to define
the
semi-infinite homology
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
with coefficients in
M

[
14
, Sections 3.8.19–22]
as the homology of a natural
differential on
\bigwedge^{\infty/2}(V)\otimes_{k}M
(see
[
14
, the beginning
of Section 3.8]
for a historical discussion).
In addition, in
[
67
, Sections D.5.5–6]
, the semi-infinite

cohomology
of a Tate Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
with coefficients in any

{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}
-contramodule

{\mathfrak{P}}
where
1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}}
acts
by the identity (“a
{\mathfrak{g}}
-contramodule with the central
charge
\varkappa_{0}
”) is also defined as the cohomology of a natural
differential on the space
\Hom_{k}(\bigwedge^{\infty/2}(V),\medspace{\mathfrak{P}})
.

In particular, for the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}=k((z))d/dz
of vector fields
on the formal circle, the canonical central extension
{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}

is the Virasoro algebra
\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
(see Section
1.7
).
The canonical basis element
1\in k\subset\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
is
-C/26
, i. e.,
the central element
C\in\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
acts by the scalar
-26
in the space
of semi-infinite forms
\bigwedge^{\infty/2}\big(k((z))d/dz\big)
.
So the semi-infinite homology and cohomology is defined for any
discrete
\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
-module (or, as we will say, “
k((z))d/dz
-module”)
with the central charge
C=26
and any
\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
-contramodule
(“
k((z))d/dz
-contramodule”) with the central charge
-26
.

When a Tate vector space
V
is endowed with a continuous coaction
of a commutative Hopf algebra
{\mathcal{C}}
, the topological Lie algebra

\gl(V)^{~}
acquires the induced continuous coaction of
{\mathcal{C}}

[
67
, Sections D.1.6–7]
.
Hence a continuous coaction of a commutative Hopf algebra
{\mathcal{C}}

in a Tate Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
always lifts naturally to a continuous
coaction of
{\mathcal{C}}
in the canonical central extension
{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
Furthermore, the canonical central extension
{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
splits
naturally over any compact open Lie subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}

[
14
, Section 3.8.19]
,
[
67
, Section D.1.8]
(warning:
over a pair of embedded compact open Lie subalgebras
{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
, these splittings do
not
agree, but rather
differ by a relative adjoint trace character).
For any Tate Harish-Chandra pair
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
, this allows to extend
the canonical central extension
\varkappa_{0}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}^{~}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
of
the Tate Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
to a canonical central extension of Tate
Harish-Chandra pairs
({\mathfrak{g}}^{~},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
, which we will
denote also by
\varkappa_{0}
.

Let
\varkappa\colon({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
be a central extension of
Tate Harish-Chandra pairs with the kernel
k=k\cdot 1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
.
By a
Harish-Chandra module

{\mathcal{M}}

over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
with
the central charge
\varkappa
we mean a Harish-Chandra module
over the Tate Harish-Chandra pair
({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})
such that the central
element
1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}\in{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
acts by the identity operator in
{\mathcal{M}}
.
Similarly, a
Harish-Chandra contramodule

{\mathfrak{P}}
over
a Tate Harish-Chandra pair
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

with the central
charge
\varkappa
is a Harish-Chandra contramodule over
({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})

such that the central element
1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
contraacts by
the identity operator in
{\mathfrak{P}}
, that is the composition

{\mathfrak{P}}\simeq k\otimes^{^}\,{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\otimes^{^}\,{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}

of the map induced by the choice of the element
1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}\in{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
with
the
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-contraaction map is equal to the identity map
{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
.

The category
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
of Harish-Chandra modules over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

with the central charge
\varkappa
is abelian; the fully faithful embedding
functor
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})
is exact and preserves
both the infinite direct sums and products.
The category
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
of Harish-Chandra contramodules over

({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
with the central charge
\varkappa
is also abelian; the fully
faithful embedding functor
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow{\mathsf{O}}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})
is exact
and preserves the infinite direct sums and products.
There are enough injective objects in the category
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})

and enough projective objects in the category
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})
;
we will see below in Section
3.5
how one can
construct them.

Our next goal is to define semialgebras
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
such that the categories of Harish-Chandra
modules and contramodules over a Tate Harish-Chandra pair
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

with the central charge
\varkappa
could be identified with
the categories of semimodules and semicontramodules over
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}

and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}
.
Let us start with the related elementary construction of
the modified universal enveloping algebra corresponding to
a central extension of (nontopological) Lie algebras.

Given a central extension
\varkappa\colon{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
with the kernel

k=k\cdot 1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
, the algebra
U_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}})
is constructed
as the quotient algebra
U({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})/(1_{U({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})}-1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}})
of the enveloping
algebra
U({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})
by the ideal generated by the difference between
the unit element
1_{U({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})}
of the associative algebra
U({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})
and
the fixed basis vector
1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
in the kernel of the central extension.
Then the category of left
U_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}})
-modules is isomorphic to
the category of “
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules with the central charge
\varkappa
”,
i. e.,
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-modules where the element
1_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
acts by the identity
operator, while the category of right
U_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}})
-modules is
isomorphic to the category of
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules with the central
charge
-\varkappa
.

Now the semialgebras
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
are
constructed as the tensor products

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}=U_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}})\otimes_{U({\mathfrak{h}})}{\mathcal{C}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}={\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{U({\mathfrak{h}})}U_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}}).

The datum of a left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}
-semimodule structure on a given
k
-vector
space
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
is equivalent to that of a Harish-Chandra module structure
over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
with the central charge
\varkappa
, while the datum of a left

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}
-semicontramodule structure on a given
k
-vector space

{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}
is equivalent to that of a Harish-Chandra contramodule structure
over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
with the central charge
\varkappa

[
67
, Sections D.2.2, D.2.5 and D.2.8]
.
The following theorem, when its condition is satisfied, allows to
describe left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}
-semimodules.

Theorem
.

Assume that the pairing
\phi\colon{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}U({\mathfrak{h}})\longrightarrow k
is nondegenerate
in
{\mathcal{C}}
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism of semialgebras

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}_{\varkappa+\varkappa_{0}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})\simeq{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

over the coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proof.

This is one of the most difficult, and at the same time a singularly
least well-understood result of the book
[
67
]
.
The precise formulation, where the desired isomorphism is uniquely
characterized by a certain list of conditions, can be found
in
[
67
, Section D.3.1]
.
The lengthy proof, which is based on the relative nonhomogeneous
quadratic duality theory developed in
[
67
, Chapter 11]

(see
[
67
, Section 0.4]
for an introduction, cf.
[
83
]
),
occupies the rest of
[
67
, Section D.3]
.
∎

3.
Tensor Operations and Adjusted Objects

3.1.
Comodules and contramodules over coalgebras over fields

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a coassociative coalgebra over a field
k
.
The constructions of the
cotensor product

{\mathcal{N}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{M}}
of a right

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{N}}
and a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
and the vector space
of
cohomomorphisms

\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathfrak{P}})
from a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule

{\mathcal{M}}
to a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
were already presented at the end
of Section
2.5
and repeated in the beginning of
Section
2.6
.
The main function of these two tensor operations on comodules and
contramodules in the theories developed in the book
[
67
]
is
to provide the tensor and module category structures in whose terms
the notion of a semialgebra and the categories of semimodules
and semicontramodules are subsequently defined.

Let us now introduce the definition of the
contratensor
product
of a right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{N}}
and a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule

{\mathfrak{P}}
, which plays a key role in the comodule-contramodule
correspondence constructions.
The contratensor product
{\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}
is a
k
-vector space defined
as the cokernel of (the difference of) the pair of maps

({\mathrm{id}}\otimes{\mathrm{ev}}_{\mathcal{C}})\circ(\nu_{N}\otimes{\mathrm{id}}),\ \,{\mathrm{id}}\otimes\pi_{\mathfrak{P}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\colon{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}},

one of which is the composition
{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}}
of the map induced
by the
{\mathcal{C}}
-coaction in
{\mathcal{N}}
and the map induced by the evaluation
map
{\mathrm{ev}}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
, while the other one is
induced by the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contraaction in
{\mathfrak{P}}
.
The functor of contratensor product of comodules and contramodules
over a coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
is right exact.
For any right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{N}}
and
k
-vector space
V

there is a natural isomorphism of
k
-vector spaces

{\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},V)\simeq{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}V.

Furthermore, for any right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{N}}
, left

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
, and
k
-vector space
V
there is a natural
isomorphism of
k
-vector spaces

\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace V)\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{P}},\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{N}},V)),

where the vector space
\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{N}},V)
is endowed with a left

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule structure as explained in
Section
1.2

[
67
, Sections 0.2.6 and 5.1.1]
.

For any two coalgebras
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
over
k
, any

{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
-bicomodule
{\mathcal{K}}
, and any left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
,
the vector space of
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule homomorphisms

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{M}})
has a natural left
{\mathcal{D}}
-contramodule structure.
One can define it by noticing that
\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{M}})
is
a subcontramodule in the left
{\mathcal{D}}
-contramodule
\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{M}})
,
whose contramodule structure is induced by the right
{\mathcal{D}}
-comodule
structure on
{\mathcal{K}}
.
Similarly, for any
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
-bicomodule
{\mathcal{K}}
and left

{\mathcal{D}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
the vector space
{\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathfrak{P}}
has
a natural left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule structure.

For any
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
-bicomodule
{\mathcal{K}}
, left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
,
and left
{\mathcal{D}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
there is a natural isomorphism
of
k
-vector spaces

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{D}}({\mathfrak{P}},\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{M}})).

In other words, the functor
{\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{D}}{-}\colon{\mathcal{D}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
is left adjoint to the functor
\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{K}},{-})\colon{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{D}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}

[
67
, Section 5.1.2]
.
The adjoint functors
\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{C}},{-})
and
{\mathcal{C}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{-}
of
comodule homomorphisms from and contratensor product with
the
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{C}}
-bicomodule
{\mathcal{K}}={\mathcal{C}}
restricted to the additive
subcategories of injective
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules and projective

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules provide the equivalence of additive categories

{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}}\simeq{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
described at the end of
Section
1.2
.

Relations between (or rather, in the case of a coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}

over a field
k
, the coincidences of) the following classes of
adjusted objects, together with the classes of injective and
projective objects, in the comodule and contramodule categories play
an important role in the co/contramodule and semico/semicontramodule
theory.
A discussion of these coincidences is the aim of the remaining part
of this section.
We will see in the proof of Proposition
3.5
below
how these results are being used.

A left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
is called
coflat
if the functor

{-}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{M}}\colon{\operatorname{\mathsf{comod--}}}{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
of cotensor product with
{\mathcal{M}}

is exact on the abelian category of right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules.
A left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
is called
coprojective
if
the functor
\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{M}},{-})\colon{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
is
exact on the abelian category of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules.

Similarly, a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
is called
contraflat

if the functor
{-}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}\penalty{\mathfrak{P}}\colon\allowbreak{\operatorname{\mathsf{comod--}}}{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
of contratensor product with
{\mathfrak{P}}
is exact on the abelian
category of right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules.
A left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
is called
coinjective
if
the functor
\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({-},{\mathfrak{P}})\colon{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
is exact
on the abelian category of left

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules
[
67
, Section 0.2.9]
.

Lemma
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a coassociative coalgebra over a field
k
. Then

(a)
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule is coflat if and only if it is
coprojective and if and only if it is injective;

(b)
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule is contraflat if and only if it is
coinjective and if and only if it is projective.

Proof.

Part (a): it is clear from the natural isomorphism

\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{N}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{M}},\medspace V)\simeq\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{M}},\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{N}},V))
for
any right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{N}}
, left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
, and

k
-vector space
V
(see Section
2.6
) that
any coprojective left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
is coflat, and from
the natural isomorphism
\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}V,\medspace{\mathfrak{P}})\simeq\Hom_{k}(V,{\mathfrak{P}})

for any
k
-vector space
V
and left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}

(see Section
2.5
) that any injective left

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
is coprojective.

Conversely, by a comodule version of Baer’s criterion,
a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
is injective whenever the functor

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({-},{\mathcal{M}})
is exact on the category of
finite-dimensional

left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules.
Indeed, a
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule morphism into
{\mathcal{M}}
from a subcomodule of
any
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule can be successively extended to larger and larger
subcomodules in the way of a transfinite induction or Zorn’s lemma,
and one only has to deal with subcomodules of finite-dimensional

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules in the process.
It remains to notice the natural right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule structure
on the dual vector space
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}
to any finite-dimensional left

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{L}}
, and the natural isomorphism

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{M}})\simeq{\mathcal{L}}^{*}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{M}}

for any finite-dimensional left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{L}}
and any left

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
, in order to conclude that any coflat left

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
is injective.

Part (b): since any
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule is a union of its
finite-dimensional subcomodules, and the functor of contratensor
product
{-}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}
preserves inductive limits (in its comodule
argument), a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
is contraflat whenever
the functor
{-}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}
is exact on the category of
finite-dimensional right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules.
It remains to notice the natural isomorphism

\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{L}},{\mathfrak{P}})\simeq{\mathcal{L}}^{*}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}

for any finite-dimensional left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{L}}
and any left

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
in order to conclude that any coinjective
left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule is contraflat.
The assertion that every projective
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule is
coinjective follows immediately from the natural isomorphism

\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{M}},\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},V))\simeq\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{M}},V)
for any left

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
and
k
-vector space
V
(see
Section
2.5
).

Showing that every contraflat
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule is projective
is much more difficult.
This assertion was formulated as a conjecture in
[
66
]

and eventually proven in
[
67
, Section A.3]
.
The result in question is a generalization of the classical theorem
that flat modules over a finite-dimensional associative algebra are
projective
[
6
, Theorem P and Examples I.3(1)]
.
The argument is
[
6
]
is based on the structure theory of
Artinian rings.
Similarly, the proof of projectivity of contraflat contramodules
in
[
67
]
is based on the structure theory of coalgebras over
fields
[
102
, Sections 9.0–1]
and the structure theory of
contramodules over them developed in
[
67
, Section A.2]
, and
first of all, on the contramodule Nakayama lemma
(see Section
2.1
).

In the exposition below, we restrict ourselves to proving that
any coinjective
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule is projective.
This is the result that has an important application to
semicontramodules that will be considered in
Section
3.5
.
The argument that we describe here also has an advantage of being
generalizable to comodules and contramodules over corings over
arbitrary noncommutative rings
[
67
, Lemma 5.2]
.

The assertion comes out as an unexpected consequence of one of
the results establishing mutual associativity of the cotensor
product/cohomomorphism operations
\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}
or
\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}
with
the operations of contratensor product, comodule homomorphisms,
or contramodule homomorphisms
\odot_{\mathcal{C}}
,
\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}
, or
\Hom^{\mathcal{C}}
.
The operations from the first and the second list are exact on
different sides, so they are only mutually associative under certain
adjustedness conditions on the objects
involved
[
67
, Section 5.2]
(cf.
[
66
]
, where these
mutual associativity assertions were formulated in a less general
form insufficient for deducing the corollary under discussion).

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
be two coalgebras over a field
k
.

Proposition 1
.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a right
{\mathcal{D}}
-comodule,
{\mathcal{K}}
be
a
{\mathcal{D}}
-
{\mathcal{C}}
-bicomodule, and
{\mathfrak{P}}
be a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule.
Then there is a natural map of
k
-vector spaces

({\mathcal{N}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{K}})\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{N}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}),

which is an isomorphism whenever
{\mathfrak{P}}
is a contraflat left

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule or
{\mathcal{N}}
is a coflat right
{\mathcal{D}}
-comodule.

Proposition 2
.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a left
{\mathcal{D}}
-comodule,
{\mathcal{K}}
be
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{D}}
-bicomodule, and
{\mathcal{M}}
be a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule.
Then there is a natural map of
k
-vector spaces

\Cohom_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{L}},\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{M}}))\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{K}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{L}},\medspace{\mathcal{M}}),

which is an isomorphism whenever
{\mathcal{M}}
is an injective left

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule or
{\mathcal{L}}
is a coprojective left
{\mathcal{D}}
-comodule.

Proposition 3
.

Let
{\mathfrak{P}}
be a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule,
{\mathcal{K}}
be
a
{\mathcal{D}}
-
{\mathcal{C}}
-bicomodule, and
{\mathfrak{Q}}
be a left
{\mathcal{D}}
-contramodule.
Then there is a natural map of
k
-vector spaces

\Cohom_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace{\mathfrak{Q}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Hom^{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{P}},\Cohom_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathfrak{Q}})),

which is an isomorphism whenever
{\mathfrak{P}}
is a projective left

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule or
{\mathfrak{Q}}
is a coinjective left

{\mathcal{D}}
-contramodule.

Proof.

To construct the natural map in Proposition 1, one considers
the composition

({\mathcal{N}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{K}})\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{K}}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}({\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}})

and observes that it is has equal compositions with the two maps

({\mathcal{N}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{K}})\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\rightrightarrows({\mathcal{N}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{K}})\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}}
,
as well as with the two maps

{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}({\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}})\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{D}}\otimes_{k}({\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}})
.
This map is an isomorphism when the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}

is contraflat, since the exact sequence

0\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{N}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{K}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{K}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{D}}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{K}}

remains exact after applying the functor
{-}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}
,
and when the
{\mathcal{D}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{N}}
is coflat, since the exact
sequence

{\mathcal{K}}\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{K}}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}0

remains exact after applying the functor
{\mathcal{N}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{D}}{-}
.

To construct the natural map in Proposition 3, one considers
the composition

\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace{\mathfrak{Q}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{K}}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace{\mathfrak{Q}})\\
\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\simeq\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Hom_{k}({\mathfrak{P}},\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathfrak{Q}}))\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Hom_{k}({\mathfrak{P}},\Cohom_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathfrak{Q}}))

and observes that it has equal compositions with the two maps

\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{D}}\otimes_{k}({\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}),\medspace\allowbreak{\mathfrak{Q}})\rightrightarrows\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace{\mathfrak{Q}})
,
as well as with the two maps
\Hom_{k}({\mathfrak{P}},\Cohom_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathfrak{Q}}))\allowbreak\rightrightarrows\Hom_{k}(\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}}),\Cohom_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathfrak{Q}}))
.
This map is an isomorphism when the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
is
projective, since the exact sequence

\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{D}}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{K}},\medspace{\mathfrak{Q}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathfrak{Q}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Cohom_{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathfrak{Q}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}0

remains exact after applying the functor
\Hom^{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{P}},{-})
, and
when the
{\mathcal{D}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{Q}}
is coinjective, since the exact
sequence

{\mathcal{K}}\otimes_{k}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{K}}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathcal{K}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}0

remains exact after applying the functor
\Cohom_{\mathcal{D}}({-},{\mathfrak{Q}})
.
The proof of Proposition 2 is similar.
∎

Now we can prove that any coinjective left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}

is projective.
Let
l\colon{\mathfrak{E}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
be a surjective
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule morphism.
According to the second assertion of Proposition 3 applied to
the coalgebras
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{D}}
, the bicomodule
{\mathcal{K}}={\mathcal{C}}
, and the contramodules

{\mathfrak{Q}}={\mathfrak{P}}
, there is a commutative diagram of maps of
k
-vector spaces
with a lower horizontal isomorphism

\dgARROWLENGTH=4em\begin{diagram}

The leftmost vertical map, being a quotient of the surjective map

\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace l)\colon\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace{\mathfrak{E}})\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace{\mathfrak{P}}),

is surjective; so the rightmost vertical map is surjective, too.
It follows that the morphism
{\mathrm{id}}\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
can be lifted to
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule morphism
{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{E}}
, i. e., our surjective
morphism of
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules
l\colon{\mathfrak{E}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
splits.
∎

3.2.
Contramodules over pro-Artinian local rings

A
pro-Artinian commutative ring

{\mathfrak{R}}
is the projective limit of
a filtered projective system of Artinian commutative rings and
surjective morphisms between them.
Equivalently,
{\mathfrak{R}}
is a complete and separated topological
commutative ring where open ideals form a base of neighborhoods of
zero and all the discrete quotient rings are
Artinian
[
69
, Section A.2]
.

A
pro-Artinian local ring
is the projective limit of a filtered
projective system of Artinian commutative local rings and surjective
morphisms between them.
E. g., any complete Noetherian local ring can be viewed as
a pro-Artinian local ring.

Contramodules over pro-Artinian local rings are suggested
in
[
69
]
for use in the role of coefficients in homological
theories involving tensor operations, infinite direct sums and products,
and reductions to the residue field.
The point is that the use of contramodules with their well-behaved
reductions (satisfying Nakayama’s lemma irrespectively of any finite
generatedness assumptions) allows to extend to pro-Artinian local rings
many results originally provable over a field only.

Let
{\mathfrak{R}}
be a pro-Artinian local ring with the maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
the residue field
k
.
The category of
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
and the reduction
functor
{\mathfrak{P}}\longmapsto{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{P}}
acting from it to the category of

k
-vector spaces have the following formal properties:

(i)

{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
is an abelian category with infinite direct sums
and products; the infinite product functors in
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
are exact
and preserved by the forgetful functor
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
;

(ii)

{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
is a tensor category with a right exact tensor
product functor
\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}
and an internal Hom functor
\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}
;
the forgetful functor
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
takes the unit
object of the tensor structure
{\mathfrak{R}}\in{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
to the unit object

{\mathfrak{R}}\in{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
and commutes with the internal Hom functors;

(iii)

there are enough projective objects in
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
; these are
called the
free
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
, as they are precisely
the direct sums of copies of the object
{\mathfrak{R}}
; the class of free

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules is preserved by infinite direct sums, infinite
products, and the operations
\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}
and
\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}
;

(iv)

the reduction functor
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow k{\operatorname{\mathsf{--vect}}}
preserves
infinite direct sums, infinite products, commutes with the tensor
products, and commutes with the internal Hom from free

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules;

(v)

the reduction functor does not annihilate any objects:

{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{P}}=0
implies
{\mathfrak{P}}=\penalty 0
for any
{\mathfrak{P}}\in{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
;
in other words, a morphism of free
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{G}}

is an isomorphism whenever its reduction
{\mathfrak{F}}/{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{G}}/{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{G}}
is
an isomorphism of
k
-vector spaces.

The constructions of these structures and the proofs of the listed
assertions can be found in
[
69
, Sections 1.1–1.6]
.
Let us point out two caveats, which are in fact two ways to formulate
one and the same observation.
Firstly, the infinite direct sums of
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules are
not

always exact functors.
Secondly, the functors of tensor product
{\mathfrak{F}}\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}{-}
with a free

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{F}}
is
not
always exact in
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
.

Both problems do not occur in the homological dimension
1
case.
E. g., in the case of the ring of
l
-adic integers
{\mathfrak{R}}={\mathbb{Z}}_{l}

or the ring of formal power series in one variable
{\mathfrak{R}}=k[[z]]
,
the infinite direct sums in
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
are still exact, as are
the tensor products with free
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules.
Of course, even in these cases the infinite direct sums and
tensor products of
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules are not preserved
by the forgetful functor
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
(and one would
not expect them to be).

The definition of a left contramodule over a complete and separated
topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
with open right ideals forming a base of
neighborhoods of zero was explained in Section
2.1
.
Let us now define the operation of tensor product
\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}
of
contramodules over a commutative topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
with open
ideals forming a base of neighborhoods of zero.

The following definition of a contrabilinear map for contramodules
over a commutative topological ring was suggested to the author
by Deligne.
Let
{\mathfrak{P}}
,
{\mathfrak{Q}}
, and
{\mathfrak{K}}
be three contramodules over
{\mathfrak{R}}
.
A map
b\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\times{\mathfrak{Q}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{K}}
is called
contrabilinear
over

{\mathfrak{R}}
if for any two families of coefficients
r_{\alpha}
and
s_{\beta}\in{\mathfrak{R}}

converging to zero in the topology of
{\mathfrak{R}}
and any two families of
elements
p_{\alpha}\in{\mathfrak{P}}
and
q_{\beta}\in{\mathfrak{Q}}
the equation

b\left(\sum_{\alpha}r_{\alpha}p_{\alpha},\medspace\sum_{\beta}s_{\beta}q_{\beta}\right)=\sum_{\alpha,\beta}(r_{\alpha}s_{\beta})\,b(p_{\alpha},q_{\beta})

holds in
{\mathfrak{K}}
.
An
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{L}}
endowed with an
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contrabilinear map

{\mathfrak{P}}\times{\mathfrak{Q}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{L}}
is called the
contramodule tensor product

of the
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{Q}}
if for any
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule

{\mathfrak{K}}
and any
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contrabilinear map
{\mathfrak{P}}\times{\mathfrak{Q}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{K}}
there
exists a unique
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule morphism
{\mathfrak{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{K}}
making
the triangle diagram
{\mathfrak{P}}\times{\mathfrak{Q}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{K}}

commutative
[
69
, Section 1.6]
.

One has to check that, for any
{\mathfrak{R}}
-subcontramodules
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{P}}

and
{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{Q}}
, any
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contrabilinear map
{\mathfrak{P}}\times{\mathfrak{Q}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{K}}

annihilating
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\times{\mathfrak{Q}}
and
{\mathfrak{P}}\times{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\prime}
factorizes uniquely
through a contrabilinear map
{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\times{\mathfrak{Q}}/{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{K}}
.
Then it follows that the contramodule tensor product
{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{Q}}/{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\prime}
can be obtained as the cokernel of the natural morphism
of contramodule tensor products

{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{Q}}\,\oplus\,{\mathfrak{P}}\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\prime}\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}{\mathfrak{P}}\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{Q}},

so in order to produce the contramodule tensor products of arbitrary
pairs of
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules it remains to explain what
the tensor products of free
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules are.
Here one notices that setting
{\mathfrak{R}}[[X]]\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{R}}[[Y]]\simeq{\mathfrak{R}}[[X\times Y]]
for any two sets
X
and
Y
does the job.
Moreover, one has
{\mathfrak{R}}[[X]]\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{P}}\simeq\bigoplus_{X}{\mathfrak{P}}
for any set

X
and any
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
, and generally the contramodule
tensor product is a right exact functor preserving infinite direct
sums in the category
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
.

The set
\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}({\mathfrak{P}},{\mathfrak{Q}})
of all
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule morphisms between
two
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{Q}}
is endowed with
the
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule structure provided by the pointwise infinite
summation operations

\left(\sum_{\alpha}r_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\right)(p)=\sum_{\alpha}r_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}(p)

for any family of morphisms
f_{\alpha}\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{Q}}
, any element

p\in{\mathfrak{P}}
, and any family of coefficients
r_{\alpha}\in{\mathfrak{R}}
converging
to zero in the topology of
{\mathfrak{R}}

[
69
, Section 1.5]
.
One easily checks from the definitions that

\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}({\mathfrak{P}}\otimes^{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{Q}},\medspace{\mathfrak{K}})\simeq\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}({\mathfrak{P}},\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}({\mathfrak{Q}},{\mathfrak{K}}))

for any
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{P}}
,
{\mathfrak{Q}}
, and
{\mathfrak{K}}
, as is required
of the internal Hom functor in a tensor category.

Given an
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
, one denotes by
{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{P}}\subset{\mathfrak{P}}

the image of the contraaction map
{\mathfrak{R}}[[{\mathfrak{P}}]]\supset{\mathfrak{m}}[[{\mathfrak{P}}]]\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
.
The passage to the quotient
k
-vector space
{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{P}}
provides
the construction of the reduction functor
{\mathfrak{P}}\longmapsto{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{P}}
.

We have explained the constructions of all the structures mentioned
in (i-v).
The proof of the assertion that the class of projective

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules is closed under the operations of infinite product
and the internal Hom functor depends on the assumption that
the ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
is pro-Artinian
[
69
, Lemma 1.3.7]
, as does
the proof of the fact that the reduction functor preserves
infinite products
[
69
, Lemma 1.3.6]
.
The proof of the Nakayama lemma (v) is based on the assumption of
topological nilpotence of the ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}
implied by the definition
of a pro-Artinial local ring (see Section
2.1
),
and the assertion that all projective
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules are free
follows from the Nakayama lemma
[
69
, Lemma 1.3.2]
.

In addition to
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules, the coefficient formalism developed
in
[
69
, Section 1]
also includes
discrete
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules

or
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules
; see Section
3.6
for
a short discussion and
[
69
, Sections 1.4 and 1.9]
for
the details.

3.3.
Flat contramodules over topological rings

The aim of this section is to describe a certain class of adjusted
contramodules over topological rings which plays a crucial role in
the arguments in
[
88
, Section 5]
and
[
89
, Section 7]
,
and will probably prove to be important and useful in other contexts
as well (cf.
[
80
,
92
,
9
,
10
,
93
]
).
To begin with, let us briefly return to the discussion of contramodules
over the adic completions of Noetherian rings by centrally generated
ideals from Section
2.2
.

Let
I
be an ideal generated by central elements in a right
Noetherian ring
R
; denote by
{\mathfrak{R}}=\varprojlim_{n}R/I^{n}

the
I
-adic completion of the ring
R
, viewed as a complete
and separated topological ring in its natural topology.
Let
{\mathfrak{I}}=\varprojlim_{n}I/I^{n}
denote the ideal generated by
the image of
I\subset R
in the ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
.

The following result is
[
70
, Proposition C.5.4]

or
[
93
, Lemma 10.2 and proof of Proposition 5.5 in Section 10]
.
In the commutative case, its proof can be found
in
[
69
, Lemma B.9.2]
or
[
76
, Corollary 10.3]
.

Lemma 1
.

A left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
is a flat
R
-module if and only
if the
R/I^{n}
-module
{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{I}}^{n}{\mathfrak{P}}
is flat for every
n\geqslant 1
.
The natural map
{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{I}}^{n}{\mathfrak{P}}
is an isomorphism
if this is the case. ∎

In other words, the nonseparatedness phenomenon demonstrated by
the counterexamples from
[
99
,
110
,
67
]
described
in Section
1.5
above does not occur for

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules satisfying any one of the two equivalent flatness
conditions from the first sentence of Lemma.
The situation turns out to be similar in the much more general
contexts discussed below.

Let us now pass to the setting of
[
70
, Section D.1]
.
Let
R_{0}\longleftarrow R_{1}\longleftarrow R_{2}\longleftarrow R_{3}\longleftarrow\dotsb
be
a projective system of associative rings and surjective morphisms
between them.
Consider the projective limit
{\mathfrak{R}}=\varprojlim_{n}R_{n}
and endow it
with the topology of projective limit of discrete rings
R_{n}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
denote the kernels of the natural surjective
morphisms of rings
{\mathfrak{R}}\longrightarrow R_{n}
; then the open ideals
{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}
form
a base of neighborhoods of zero in the topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
.
To make our notation closer to that of
[
70
, Appendix D]

and
[
86
]
, we switch a bit away from our previous notation
pattern and denote by
{\mathfrak{J}}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{P}}\subset{\mathfrak{P}}
the image of the contraaction
map
{\mathfrak{J}}[[{\mathfrak{P}}]]\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
for any closed ideal
{\mathfrak{J}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
and any

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
.

We recall from Section
2.1
that the category
of left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
is an abelian category with
infinite direct sums, exact infinite products, and enough projective
objects.
The next result is
[
70
, Lemma D.1.1]
; see
also
[
67
, Lemma A.2.3]
.

Lemma 2
.

For any left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
, the natural map

{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{P}}
is surjective. ∎

Here is the promised definition in this context.
A left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{F}}
is called
flat
if
the left
R_{n}
-module
{\mathfrak{F}}/{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{F}}
is flat for every
n
.
The following proposition is the main related result.

Proposition 1
.

(a)
For any flat left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{F}}
, the map

{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}{\mathfrak{F}}/{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{F}}
is an isomorphism.

(b)
The class of flat left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules is closed
under extensions and the passages to the kernels of surjective
morphisms.
For any short exact sequence of flat
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules

0\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{G}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow 0
, the short sequences
of
R_{n}
-modules
0\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{H}}/{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{G}}/{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{G}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}}/{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow 0
are exact.

Proof.

Part (a) is
[
70
, Corollary D.1.7]
.
Part (b) is
[
70
, Lemmas D.1.4 and D.1.5]
.
∎

The next result provides a characterization of projective

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules, generalizing the results
of
[
110
, Corollary 4.5]
and
[
69
, Corollary B.8.2]

(see also
[
65
, Theorem 1.10]

and
[
70
, Corollary C.5.6(a)]
).

Proposition 2
.

A left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{F}}
is a projective object in

{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
if and only if the left
{\mathfrak{R}}_{n}
-modules
{\mathfrak{F}}/{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{F}}

are projective for all
n\geqslant 0
.

Proof.

This is
[
70
, Corollary D.1.10(a)]
.
∎

Finally, we consider the quite general setting of a complete, separated
topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
with a countable base of neighborhoods of zero
consisting of open right ideals
[
86
, Sections 5–7]
.
The next lemma is
[
86
, Lemma 6.3(b)]
.

Lemma 3
.

For any left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
, the natural map

{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{I}}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{P}}
, where the projective limit is
taken over the poset of open right ideals
{\mathfrak{I}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
ordered by
the inverse inclusion, is surjective. ∎

Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a discrete right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-module and
{\mathfrak{P}}
be a left

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule.
Then the
contratensor product

{\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{P}}
is an abelian
group constructed as the quotient group of
{\mathcal{N}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathfrak{P}}
by
the subgroup of all elements of the form

\sum_{\alpha}xr_{\alpha}\otimes p_{\alpha}-x\otimes\sum_{\alpha}r_{\alpha}p_{\alpha}.

Here
r_{\alpha}\in{\mathfrak{R}}
is family of elements of converging to zero in
the topology of
{\mathfrak{R}}
, indexed by some family of indices
\alpha

(which can be assumed to be at most countable when
{\mathfrak{R}}
has a countable
base of neighborhoods of zero),
p_{\alpha}\in{\mathfrak{P}}
is an arbitrary family
of elements, and
x\in{\mathcal{N}}
is an element.
The sum in the left-hand side is well-defined since
xr_{\alpha}=0
for
all but a finite subset of indices
\alpha
, while the sum in
the right-hand side denotes the contramodule infinite summation
operation.

For any discrete right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-module
{\mathcal{N}}
, any left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule

{\mathfrak{P}}
, and any abelian group
V
, there is a natural isomorphism of
abelian groups

\Hom_{\mathsf{Ab}}({\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{P}},\medspace V)\simeq\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}({\mathfrak{P}},\Hom_{\mathsf{Ab}}({\mathcal{N}},V)),

where the left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule structure on the abelian group

\Hom_{\mathsf{Ab}}({\mathcal{N}},V)
is constructed as explained in
Section
2.1
.
For any set
X
and any discrete right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-module
{\mathcal{N}}
, there is
a natural isomorphism of abelian groups

{\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{R}}[[X]]\simeq{\mathcal{N}}[X].

Here is our key definition.
A left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{F}}
is called
flat
if the functor
of contratensor product
{\mathcal{N}}\longmapsto{\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{F}}
is exact on
the abelian category of discrete right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules
{\mathcal{N}}
.
For a (complete and separated) topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
with a countable
base of neighborhoods of zero consisting of open
two-sided

ideals, this definition is equivalent to the previous one.
The following proposition lists some of the main results of
the theory.

Proposition 3
.

(a)
For any flat left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{F}}
, the map

{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{F}}/{\mathfrak{I}}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{F}}
is an isomorphism.

(b)
All projective left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules are flat.
The class of flat left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules is closed
under extensions and the passages to the kernels of surjective
morphisms.
For any short exact sequence of flat left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules

0\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{G}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow 0
and any discrete right

{\mathfrak{R}}
-module
{\mathcal{N}}
, the short sequence of abelian groups

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{G}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}\odot_{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow 0

is exact.

(c)
The class of flat left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules is closed
under filtered inductive limits in
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
.
The functor of filtered inductive limit of diagrams of flat left

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules is exact.

Proof.

Part (a) is
[
86
, Corollary 6.15]
.
Part (b) is
[
86
, Lemma 6.7, Corollary 6.8, Lemma 6.9, and
Corollary 6.13]
(see also
[
86
, Lemma 6.10]
).
Part (c) is
[
86
, Lemmas 5.6 and 6.16]
(see
also
[
86
, Proposition 6.17]
).
∎

An even more general setting of contramodules over a complete,
separated topological ring with a (not necessarily countable) base
of neighborhoods of zero consisting of open right ideals is
discussed in the papers
[
80
,
92
,
10
,
93
]
.
In this context, the definition of a flat contramodule is the same
as in the previous one.
The class of flat contramodules is still closed under filtered
inductive limits, but it
no
longer needs to be closed under
the kernels of surjective morphisms
[
93
, Example 12.4]
.
An example of a flat contramodule which cannot be obtained as
a filtered inductive limit of projective ones can be found
in
[
93
, Example 9.2]
.
Topological rings over which the classes of projective and flat
left contramodules coincide are characterized, under the name of

topologically left perfect
topological rings, in
the paper
[
92
, Section 14]
.

3.4.
Underived co-contra correspondence over corings

It was already mentioned in the end of Section
1.2

that the categories of
injective
left
comodules
and

projective
left
contramodules
over a coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
over
a field
k
are naturally equivalent.
Similarly, at the end of Section
1.4
we pointed out
the equivalence between the categories of injective discrete modules
and projective contramodules over the ring of
l
-adic integers
{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}
.
These are the simplest instances of a very general homological
phenomenon called the
comodule-contramodule correspondence
,
which has many manifestations in
algebra
[
59
,
32
,
64
,
50
,
56
,
48
,
68
,
69
,
72
,
73
]
,
algebraic geometry
[
62
,
61
,
33
,
70
]
, and representation
theory
[
37
,
38
,
96
,
67
,
90
,
91
]
.
In the following three sections we restrict ourselves to an overview of
those versions of the co-contra correspondence that can be readily
formulated on the level of
additive
or
exact
categories,
while referring to the presentation
[
71
]
and the introduction to
the paper
[
72
]
for a discussion of the derived
comodule-contramodule correspondence.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a coassociative coring over an associative ring
A

(see Section
2.5
).
Then the assignment of the left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
\Hom_{A}({\mathcal{C}},V)
to
the left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{A}V
and vice versa establishes
an equivalence between the full additive subcategories of
coinduced
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules in
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
and induced

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules in
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
.
Indeed, one has

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{A}U,\medspace{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{A}V)\simeq\Hom_{A}({\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{A}U,\medspace V)\\
\simeq\Hom_{A}(U,\Hom_{A}({\mathcal{C}},V))\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{C}}(\Hom_{A}({\mathcal{C}},U),\Hom_{A}({\mathcal{C}},V))

for any left
A
-modules
U
and
V

[
67
, Section 0.2.6]
.
This is a particular case of the isomorphism of
Kleisli
categories
for a pair (left adjoint comonad, right adjoint monad)
in any base category
[
53
,
25
]
.

Adjoining the direct summands to the full subcategories of
coinduced
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules and induced
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules, one
obtains what are called the full subcategories of relatively
injective (or
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective)
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules and relatively
projective
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules in
the book
[
22
, Sections 18.17–18]
and
the paper
[
25
, Sections 2.7–8]
.
This may be the standard terminology; still in
the monograph
[
67
, Section 5.1.3]
we chose to call these

quite relatively injective
(quite
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective)

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules and
quite relatively projective

(quite
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective)
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules, while preserving
the shorter terms with the single word “relatively” for
the wider and more important classes of comodules and
contramodules discussed below.

A left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{J}}
is said to be
quite

{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective
if the short sequence of abelian groups

(8)

0\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{J}})\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{J}})\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{J}})\longrightarrow 0

is exact for every short exact sequence of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow 0
that
splits
as
a short exact sequence of left
A
-modules
.
We recall that the category of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules is not abelian
in general, there being a problem with the kernels of morphisms
(see Section
2.5
).
However, any
A
-split surjection of
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules has
a kernel preserved by the forgetful functor
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\longrightarrow A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
.

Moreover, the category of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules with the class of
all
A
-split short exact sequences is an exact category
(see
[
26
]
for the definition, discussion, and references);
the quite
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules are simply
the injective objects of this exact category structure.
In particular, the coaction morphism
{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{A}{\mathcal{M}}
of
any left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{M}}
is split by the
A
-module map

{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{A}{\mathcal{M}}\allowbreak\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}
induced by the counit
\varepsilon

of the coring
{\mathcal{C}}
.
Considering the corresponding
A
-split short exact sequence of

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules, one easily concludes that a
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule is
quite
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective if and only if it is a direct summand of
a coinduced
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule.

Similarly, a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{F}}
is said to be
quite

{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective
if the short sequence of abelian groups

(9)

0\longrightarrow\Hom^{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{F}},{\mathfrak{P}})\longrightarrow\Hom^{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{F}},{\mathfrak{Q}})\longrightarrow\Hom^{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{F}},{\mathfrak{R}})\longrightarrow 0

is exact for every short exact sequence of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules

0\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{Q}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}\longrightarrow 0
that
splits
as
a short exact sequence of left
A
-modules.
Recall that the category of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules is not abelian
in general, there being a problem with the cokernels of morphisms.
However, any
A
-split embedding of
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules has
