a cokernel preserved by the forgetful functor
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
.

Moreover, the category of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules with the class of
all
A
-split short exact sequences is an exact category;
the quite
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules are simply
the projective objects of this exact category structure.
In particular, the contraaction morphism
\Hom_{A}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}

of any
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
is split by the
A
-module map

{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow\Hom_{A}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})
induced by the counit of the coring
{\mathcal{C}}
.
Considering the corresponding
A
-split short exact sequence of

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules, one easily concludes that a
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
is quite
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective if and only if it is a direct summand of
an induced
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule.

So the full additive subcategories of quite
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules in
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
and quite
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules in
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
are equivalent for any
coassociative coring
{\mathcal{C}}
.
When the coring
{\mathcal{C}}
coincides with the ring
A
(i. e.,
the counit map
{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow A
is bijective), this reduces to
the identity equivalence of the category of left
A
-modules
with itself.
However, the category of
A
-modules is abelian and not only
additive; viewing it just as an additive category is rather
unsatisfactory from our point of view.
Still the categories of quite relatively injective comodules and
quite relatively projective contramodules do not seem to carry
any homological structures beyond those of additive categories;
in particular, they do
not
have any nontrivial exact category
structures.
The following definitions
[
67
, Sections 5.1.4 and 5.3]

are purported to overcome this drawback (cf.
[
17
, Sections 4.1
and 4.3]
, where similarly defined relatively adjusted
modules are called, more in line with the traditional
terminology, “weakly relatively projective” and “weakly
relatively injective”).

Assume that the coring
{\mathcal{C}}
is a projective left and a flat right

A
-module; then the categories of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules and
left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules are abelian.
A left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{J}}
is called
injective relative
to
A
(
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective) if the short sequence of Hom
groups (
8
) is exact for any short
exact sequence of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules
0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow 0
that are
projective as left
A
-modules
.
Similarly, a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{F}}
is called
projective
relative to
A
(
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective) if the short sequence of Hom
groups (
9
) is exact for any short
exact sequence of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules
0\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{Q}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}\longrightarrow 0
that are
injective as left
A
-modules
.

Assume further that the ring
A
has finite left homological dimension
(i. e., the category of left
A
-modules has finite homological
dimension; cf. the last sentence of Section
2.6
).
Then the full subcategory
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}/A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
of

{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules is closed under extensions and
the passages to the cokernels of injective morphisms in
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
.
Similarly, the full subcategory
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}/A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
of

{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules is closed under extensions
and the passages to the kernels of subjective morphisms in
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}

[
67
, Lemma 5.3.1]
.
Being closed under extensions, the full subcategories

{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}/A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
and
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}/A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
inherit the exact
category structures of the abelian categories
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
and

{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
.
Moreover, the following strong converse assertions hold.

Lemma
.

(a)
The subcategory of
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules

{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}/A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
is the minimal full subcategory
of the abelian category
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
containing the coinduced

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules and closed under extensions and direct summands.

(b)
The subcategory of
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}/A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
is
the minimal full subcategory of the abelian category
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}

containing the induced
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules and closed under
extensions and direct summands.

Proof.

This is the strengthening of the result of
[
67
, Remark 9.1]

that one obtains by replacing the resolution technique
of
[
67
, Lemma 9.1.2]
with that of
[
35
, second half of
the proof of Theorem 10]
; see also
[
70
, Corollary B.2.4]
.

To be more specific, let us prove part (b).
Let
{\mathfrak{P}}
be a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule.
The
{\mathcal{C}}
-contraaction map
{\mathfrak{G}}=\Hom_{A}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
is a surjective
morphism of
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules; let us denote its kernel by
{\mathfrak{K}}
.
According to
[
67
, Lemma 3.1.3(b)]
, there exists an injective

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule morphism
{\mathfrak{K}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{E}}
from
{\mathfrak{K}}
into
an
A
-injective left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{E}}
such that the quotient
contramodule
{\mathfrak{E}}/{\mathfrak{K}}
is a finitely iterated extension of induced

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules.
Denote by
{\mathfrak{F}}
the fibered coproduct
({\mathfrak{E}}\oplus{\mathfrak{G}})/{\mathfrak{K}}
of
the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{E}}
and
{\mathfrak{G}}
over
{\mathfrak{K}}
; then
{\mathfrak{F}}
is
an extension of the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{E}}/{\mathfrak{K}}
and
{\mathfrak{G}}
, and
there is a surjective morphism
{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
with the kernel
{\mathfrak{E}}
.

Now suppose that the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
is
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective.
Then the Ext group
\Ext^{{\mathcal{C}},\text{$\mskip 1.5mu$}1}({\mathfrak{P}},{\mathfrak{E}})
in the abelian category

{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
vanishes by
[
67
, Lemma 5.3.1(b)]
, hence
the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
is a direct summand of a finitely
iterated extension of induced
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{F}}
.
Notice that the length of the iterated extension in this construction
is bounded by the left homological dimension of the ring
A
.
∎

According to
[
67
, Theorem 5.3]
, the exact categories of

{\mathcal{C}}/A
-injective left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules and
{\mathcal{C}}/A
-projective left

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules are naturally equivalent

{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}/A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}\simeq{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}/A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}.

The equivalence is provided by the functor
\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}\colon{\mathcal{M}}\longmapsto\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})
of
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule homomorphisms from the left

{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{C}}
and the functor
\Phi_{\mathcal{C}}\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\longmapsto{\mathcal{C}}\odot_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathfrak{P}}

of
contratensor product
of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules with
the right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{C}}

[
67
, Sections 0.2.6–7 and 5.1.1]

(cf. Section
3.1
;
see also
[
25
, Section 5]
).
This equivalence of exact categories can be viewed as an instance of
the
\infty
-tilting-cotilting correspondence

phenomenon
[
91
, Example 6.7]
.

3.5.
Underived semico-semicontra correspondence

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
be a semialgebra over a coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
over a field
k

(see Section
2.6
).
Assume that
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
is an injective left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule and an injective
right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule, so the categories of left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodules and
left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodules
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}
are abelian.
The full subcategory
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
of left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodules
that are
injective as left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules
is obviously
closed under extensions (and cokernels of injective morphisms) in

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}
, while the full subcategory
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
of
left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodules that are
projective as left

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules
is closed under extensions (and kernels of
surjective morphisms) in
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}
.
Hence the full subcategories
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
and

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
inherit the exact category structures of
the abelian categories
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}
.

According to
[
67
, Sections 0.3.7 and 6.2]
(see
also
[
66
]
), the exact categories of
{\mathcal{C}}
-injective left

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodules and
{\mathcal{C}}
-projective left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodules
are naturally equivalent.
The equivalence is provided by the functor
\Psi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\colon{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}\longmapsto\Hom_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}})
of
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule homomorphisms from the left

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
and the functor
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\colon{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}\longmapsto{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\circledcirc_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}
of (
semi
)
contratensor product
of
left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodules with the right
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}

(cf. the definition of the contratensor product over a coalgebra
in Section
3.1
).
Furthermore, the equivalence of exact categories
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}\simeq{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
forms a commutative diagram of functors with
the equivalence
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}}\simeq{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
between
the additive categories of injective left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules and
projective left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules, and the forgetful functors

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}}
and

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
,

(10)

\dgARROWLENGTH=4em\begin{diagram}

The equivalence of exact categories in the upper line of
the diagram (
10
) can be also viewed as an instance of
the
\infty
-tilting-cotilting correspondence
[
91
, Example 6.9]
.

The particular case considered in Section
2.8
,
with the semialgebra

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}_{\varkappa+\varkappa_{0}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})\simeq{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{l}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

corresponding to a central extension
\varkappa\colon({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

of Tate Harish-Chandra pairs satisfying the condition of
Theorem
2.8
, is especially notable.
In this situation we obtain the commutative diagram
of equivalences of exact/additive categories and forgetful functors

(11)

\dgARROWLENGTH=4em\begin{diagram}

where
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}\subset{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
and

{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}\subset{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
denote
the full exact subcategories of
{\mathcal{C}}
-injective Harish-Chandra
modules and
{\mathcal{C}}
-projective Harish-Chandra contramodules with
the central charge
\varkappa
.

It was pointed out by Feigin–Fuchs
[
37
]
,
[
38
, Remark 2.4]

and Meurman–Frenkel–Rocha-Caridi–Wallach
[
96
]
back in
the first half of 1980’s that the categories of Verma modules
over the Virasoro algebra on any pair of complementary levels

C=c
and
C=26-c
are anti-isomorphic.
The above result extends this classical observation to the whole
exact subcategories of
{\mathcal{C}}
-adjusted objects in the abelian
categories
{\mathsf{O}}
and
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}
over any Tate Harish-Chandra
pair satisfying the nondegeneracy condition.

Indeed, consider the Tate Harish-Chandra pair

({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})=\big(k((z))d/dz,{\mathcal{C}}(H)\big)
over a field
k
of
characteristic
0
with the pro-algebraic subgroup
H

corresponding to the compact open Lie subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}=zk[[z]]d/dz\subset k((z))d/dz
as described in the beginning of
Section
2.8
.
The group
H
acts in the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
by changing
the independent variable in the vector fields,

a^{-1}\big(f(z)d/dz\big)=f(a(z))\,d/da(z)=f(a(z))/a^{\prime}(z)\,d/dz

for all
a\in H(k)
,
f\in k((z))
.

A
Verma module
over
\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
is an
U(\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir})
-module induced
from a one-dimensional module
kv_{0}
over the compact open subalgebra

{\mathfrak{h}}\oplus kC\subset\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
.
The subalgebra
z^{2}k[[z]]d/dz\subset{\mathfrak{h}}
topologically spanned
by the basis vectors
L_{n}
with
n\geqslant 1
acts by zero in
kv_{0}
,
while the generators
C
and
L_{0}
act by certain scalars
c

and
h_{0}\in k
.
These modules belong to the categories
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
with
the respective central charges
\varkappa=c
, but, as such, do
not

play any noticeable role in our theory.
Indeed, they do
not
belong to the subcategories

{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}\subset{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
, being freely
generated as modules over a Lie subalgebra
complementary

to
{\mathfrak{h}}
in
\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
and having no particular adjustedness properties
as comodules over
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}(H)
.

The
contragredient
Verma modules are relevant for us instead.
Notice first of all that the pro-algebraic group
H
contains
a subgroup whose group of points consists of the coordinate
changes
z\longmapsto a_{1}z
multiplying the coordinate
z
with
a scalar factor
a_{1}\in k
.
The category of comodules (as well as contramodules) over
(the coalgebra of) this algebraic group, which is isomorphic to
the multiplicative group
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, is equivalent to
the category of graded
k
-vector spaces.
In particular, the Verma modules
{\mathcal{M}}(c,h_{0})
over
\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
carry
the grading by the weights of the semisimple operator
L_{0}
.

Furthermore, the discrete Lie subalgebra
\bigoplus_{n}kL_{n}\oplus C\subset\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
spanned
nontopologically
by the generators

C
and
L_{n}\in\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
has an involutive automorphism
\sigma
given
by the rules
\sigma(L_{n})=-L_{-n}
and
\sigma(C)=-C
.
The
contragredient Verma module

{\mathcal{M}}(c,h_{0})^{\vee}
is
the graded dual vector space to
{\mathcal{M}}(c,h_{0})
endowed by the induced
action of the Lie subalgebra
\bigoplus_{n}kL_{n}\oplus C
, twisted
by the automorphism
\sigma
and then extended to the whole
Lie algebra
\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
by continuity.
Both the passage to the dual module and the involution
\sigma

change the sign of the central charge; hence
{\mathcal{M}}(c,h_{0})^{\vee}

is again a
\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
-module with the central charge
c
.
So the full subcategory of Verma modules on the level
\varkappa=c

(with a varying parameter
h_{0}\in k
) in
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
is
anti-isomorphic to the full subcategory of contragredient Verma
modules in the same category
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
.

Denoting by
H_{+}\subset H
the pro-unipotent pro-algebraic subgroup
whose points are the power series
a(z)=1+a_{2}z^{2}+a_{3}z^{3}+\dotsb
with

a_{n}\in k
for
n\geqslant 2
, the contragredient Verma modules can be
described as precisely those objects of the category
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})

whose structures of
{\mathcal{C}}(H_{+})
-comodules are those of cofree
comodules with one cogenerator.
Alternatively, the contragredient Verma modules are distinguished
among all the objects of
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
by the property that
their underlying
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules are the “minimal possible”,
i. e.,
indecomposable
injective comodules.

Finally, consider the category of Harish-Chandra
contramodules

over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
with the central charge
\varkappa+\varkappa_{0}=-26+c
whose

{\mathcal{C}}(H_{+})
-contramodule structures are those of free contramodules
with one generator, or equivalently, whose underlying

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules are indecomposable projective contramodules.
This full subcategory in
{\mathsf{O}}_{\varkappa+\varkappa_{0}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
is
anti-equivalent to the category of contragredient Verma modules
with the central charge
26-c
via the linear duality functor

{\mathfrak{P}}={\mathcal{N}}^{*}=\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{N}},k)
.
It is also
equivalent
to the category of Verma modules with
the central charge
26-c
via the functor assigning to a Verma
module
{\mathcal{M}}
the
infinite product

{\mathfrak{P}}=\prod_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{M}}_{n}
of
its grading components, endowed with the
\sigma
-twisted
action of the Virasoro Lie algebra and a natural

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule structure (cf. the discussion of graded
contramodules in
[
67
, Section 11.1.1]

and
[
68
, Remark 2.2]
).

So the classical duality between the categories of Verma modules
on the complementary levels appears in our setting, after
the naïve twisting and linear duality are taken into account,
as the restriction of the equivalence of exact
categories (
11
) to the full subcategories
of objects “of the minimal possible size”.
(Cf.
[
67
, Corollary and Remark D.3.1]
, where a discussion
of these results in the
derived
comodule-contramodule
correspondence context can be found; see
also
[
67
, Sections 0.2.6–7]
for relevant counterexamples
demonstrating how exotic derived categories appear
in the derived co-contra correspondence.)

Now let us explain, as it was promised in
Sections
2.6
and
2.8
,
how to use the equivalence of exact categories (
10
)
in order to construct injective objects in the category

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}
and projective objects in the category
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}
.
As above, we assume that the semialgebra
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
is an injective left
and right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule.

Proposition
.

(a)
There are enough injective objects in the abelian
category of left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodules.
A left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule is injective if and only if it is
a direct summand of an
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule of the form

\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}(\Hom_{k}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}},V))
, where
V
is a
k
-vector space.

(b)
There are enough projective objects in the abelian
category of left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodules.
A left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule is projective if and only if it is
a direct summand of an
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule of the form

\Psi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\otimes_{k}V)
, where
V
is a
k
-vector space.

Proof.

A left semimodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
over a semialgebra
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
over a coalgebra

{\mathcal{C}}
over a field
k
is called
semiprojective
if it is
a direct summand of the
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\otimes_{k}V
for some

k
-vector space
V
.
Similarly, a left semicontramodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}
over
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
is called

semiinjective
if it is a direct summand of
the
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule
\Hom_{k}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}},V)
for some vector space
V

[
67
, Sections 3.4.3 and 9.2]
.

The semiprojective semimodules are projective objects in
the exact category of
{\mathcal{C}}
-injective left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodules,
as the functor

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}\longmapsto\Hom_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\otimes_{k}V,\medspace{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}})\simeq\Hom_{k}(V,\Psi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}))\\
\simeq\Hom_{k}(V,\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}))=\Hom_{k}(V,\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{C}},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}))\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}V,\medspace{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}})

is exact on
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
.
For any
{\mathcal{C}}
-injective left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
,
the semiaction morphism
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
is an admissible
epimorphism in
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
from a semiprojective left

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
onto
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
; so the projective
objects of the category
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
are precisely
the semiprojective semimodules.

Similarly, the semiinjective semicontramodules are injective objects
in the exact category of
{\mathcal{C}}
-projective left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodules,
as the functor

{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}\longmapsto\Hom^{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}({\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}},\medspace\Hom_{k}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}},V))\simeq\Hom_{k}(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}({\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}),V)\\
\simeq\Hom_{k}(\Phi_{\mathcal{C}}({\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}),V)\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{C}}({\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}},\medspace\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},V))

is exact on
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
.
For any
{\mathcal{C}}
-projective left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}
,
the semicontraaction morphism
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}\longrightarrow\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}},{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}})
is
an admissible monomorphism in
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
from
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}
into
a semiinjective left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule
\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}},{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}})
.
So the injective objects of the category
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
are
precisely the semiinjective semicontramodules.

The functors
\Psi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
and
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
being mutually inverse
equivalences between the exact categories
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
and

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
, it follows that the
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodules

\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}(\Hom_{k}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}},V))
and their direct summands are the injective
objects of the exact category
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
, while
the
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodules
\Psi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\otimes_{k}V)
and their direct
summands are the projective objects of the exact category

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
.
Furthermore, any left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule can be embedded into
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-injective
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule.
This assertion is provided by the combination of the construction
of
[
67
, Lemma 1.3.3]
(see also
[
66
]
) with the result
of Lemma
3.1
(a) above.

Similarly, any left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule is the quotient
contramodule of a
{\mathcal{C}}
-projective
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule.
To prove this fact, one has to combine the construction
of
[
67
, Lemma 3.3.3]
(which was present already
in
[
66
]
) with the assertion of
[
67
, Lemma 5.2 or 5.3.2]

whose proof we reproduced, in our generality of coalgebras over fields,
in Lemma
3.1
(b) above.
Therefore, any left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule can be embedded into
an
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule of the form
\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}(\Hom_{k}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}},V))
, and
any left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule is the quotient contramodule of
an
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule of the form
\Psi_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\otimes_{k}V)
.

Finally, in order to show that any injective object
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{J}}}
of
the exact category
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--inj}}}}
is also an injective
object in the abelian category
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}
, suppose that we are
given an injective morphism
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{J}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}}
from
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{J}}}
into
a left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}}
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
be an injective morphism from
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}}
into
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-injective left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semimodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
.
Then
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{J}}}
is a direct summand in
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
, so there is a projection

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{J}}}
splitting the embedding
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{J}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
.
Restricting this projection to the subsemimodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}}\subset{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{M}}}
,
we see that the embedding
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{J}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}}
also splits.

Similarly, in order to prove that any projective object
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{F}}}
of
the exact category
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--proj}}}}
is also a projective
object in the abelian category
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}
, suppose that we
are given a surjective morphism
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{Q}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{F}}}
onto
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{F}}}
from
a left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{Q}}}
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{Q}}}
be a surjective morphism onto
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{Q}}}
from
a
{\mathcal{C}}
-projective left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}
.
Then
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{F}}}
is a direct summand in
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}
, so there is a section

{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{F}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}
splitting the surjection
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{F}}}
.
Composing this section with the projection
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{Q}}}
,
we obtain a section
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{F}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{Q}}}
showing that the surjection

{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{Q}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{F}}}
also splits (cf.
[
67
, proof of Lemma 9.2.1]
).
∎

3.6.
Co-contra correspondence over topological rings

In this section we discuss generalizations of the equivalence
between the additive categories of injective comodules and projective
contramodules over a coalgebra over a field to topological rings
{\mathfrak{R}}

more complicated than the linearly compact topological algebras
(which are dual to coalgebras over fields).
For examples of
derived
co-contra correspondence over
topological rings the reader is referred to
[
70
, Sections C.1,
C.5, and D.2]
(see also
[
90
, Sections 8 and 10.3]
).

First let us suppose that
{\mathfrak{R}}
is a pro-Artinian commutative ring
(see Section
3.2
).
By the definition, an
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule
is an ind-object in
the abelian category opposite to the category of discrete

{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules of finite length
[
69
, Section 1.4]
.
There is a natural contravariant functor
{\mathcal{M}}\longmapsto{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{op}}

assigning to every
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule a pro-object in the category
of discrete
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules of finite length.
Furthermore, there is a distinguished object
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{R}})
in
the category
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
of
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules such that

{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}={\mathfrak{R}}
; the functor
{\mathcal{M}}\longmapsto{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{op}}
, viewed as
a contravariant functor from
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
to the category of
abelian groups, is represented by
{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{R}})
.
A
cofree

{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule is a direct sum of copies of
the
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{C}}
; the cofree
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules are injective,
and any
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule can be embedded into a cofree one.

According to Matlis’ duality
(see
[
58
, Corollary 4.3]
or
[
60
, Theorem 18.6]
),
choosing an injective hull of the irreducible module over
an Artinian commutative local ring
R
fixes an anti-equivalence
of the category of
R
-modules of finite length with itself.
Passing to the inductive limit of such auto-anti-equivalences over
all the discrete quotient rings of a pro-Artinian commutative
ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
, one obtains an auto-anti-equivalence of the category
of discrete
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules of finite length depending on
the choice of a “minimal injective cogenerator” of the abelian
category
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--discr}}}
, i. e., an injective hull of the direct sum
of the irreducible discrete
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules.
Hence choosing such an injective object
{\mathcal{E}}\in{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--discr}}}

identifies the category of discrete
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules with the category
of
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules; the equivalence of categories
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\simeq{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--discr}}}
takes the object
{\mathcal{C}}\in{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
to the object
{\mathcal{E}}

[
69
, Section 1.9]
.

For any pro-Artinian commutative ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
, the categories of
injective
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules and projective
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules are
naturally equivalent,
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}}\simeq{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
.
The equivalence is provided by the functor
\Psi_{\mathfrak{R}}\colon{\mathcal{M}}\longmapsto\Hom_{\mathfrak{R}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})
of
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule homomorphisms from
the
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{R}})
and the functor
\Phi_{\mathfrak{R}}\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\longmapsto{\mathcal{C}}\odot_{\mathfrak{R}}{\mathfrak{P}}
of
contratensor product
of
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
with the
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{R}})
.
It assigns the free
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{R}}[[X]]
to the cofree

{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule
\bigoplus_{X}{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{R}})
for any set
X
.
This result can be found in
[
69
, Section 1.5]
.

More generally, let
{\mathfrak{R}}
be a
right pseudo-compact

topological ring, i. e., a complete, separated topological ring
with a base of neighborhoods of zero formed by open right
ideals
{\mathfrak{J}}
for which the right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules
{\mathfrak{R}}/{\mathfrak{J}}
have
finite length
[
42
, § IV.3]
.
We define
left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules
as the ind-objects in
the abelian category opposite to the category of discrete right

{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules of finite length.
The category of left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
is anti-equivalent
to the category of
pseudo-compact right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules
,
i. e., pro-objects in the category of discrete right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules
of finite length or, which is the same, complete and separated
topological right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules with a base of neighborhoods of
zero formed by open submodules with discrete quotient modules of
finite length.
As above, we denote this anti-equivalence by
{\mathcal{M}}\longmapsto{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

There is a distinguished left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{R}})
for which

{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}={\mathfrak{R}}
; the functor
{\mathcal{M}}\longmapsto{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{op}}
, viewed as
a contravariant functor from
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
to the category of abelian
groups, is represented by
{\mathcal{C}}({\mathfrak{R}})
.
A
cofree
left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule is a direct sum of copies of
the
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule
{\mathcal{C}}
; the cofree
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules are injective,
and any left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodule can be embedded into a cofree one.
The abelian category
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
is locally
finite
[
42
, § II.4]
; and the choice of an injective
cogenerator
{\mathcal{E}}
in any locally finite abelian category
{\mathsf{A}}
fixes
an equivalence between
{\mathsf{A}}
and the category of left comodules
over the topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}=\End_{\mathsf{A}}({\mathcal{E}})^{\mathrm{op}}
opposite to the ring
of endomorphisms of the object
{\mathcal{E}}\in{\mathsf{A}}
.
The topology on the ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
is defined to have a base of
neighborhoods of zero consisting of (the right ideals opposite to)
the annihilators of subobjects of finite length
{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
.
The equivalence of categories
{\mathsf{A}}\simeq{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
assigns
the object
{\mathcal{C}}\in{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
to the object
{\mathcal{E}}

[
42
, Corollaire VI.4.1]
.

For any right pseudo-compact topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
, the categories
of injective left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules and projective left

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules are naturally equivalent,

{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}_{\mathsf{inj}}\simeq{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}.

Indeed, the injective left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules are the direct summands
of the cofree
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules
\bigoplus_{X}{\mathcal{C}}(R)
, and the projective
left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules are the direct summands of the free

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
{\mathfrak{R}}[[X]]
, where
X
denotes arbitrary sets.
It remains to compute the groups of morphisms
between the cofree
{\mathfrak{R}}
-comodules and the free
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules
in terms of projective limits over the subcomodules of finite
length
{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}
and the open right ideals
{\mathfrak{J}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
,

\textstyle\Hom_{\mathfrak{R}}\big(\bigoplus_{X}{\mathcal{C}},\medspace\bigoplus_{Y}{\mathcal{C}}\big)\simeq\prod_{X}\Hom_{\mathfrak{R}}\big({\mathcal{C}},\medspace\bigoplus_{Y}{\mathcal{C}}\big)\simeq\prod_{X}\varprojlim_{{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}}\Hom_{\mathfrak{R}}\big({\mathcal{L}},\medspace\bigoplus_{Y}{\mathcal{C}}\big)\\
\textstyle\simeq\prod_{X}\varprojlim_{{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}}\bigoplus_{Y}\Hom_{\mathfrak{R}}({\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{C}})\simeq\prod_{X}\varprojlim_{{\mathfrak{J}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}}\bigoplus_{Y}{\mathfrak{R}}/{\mathfrak{J}}=\prod_{X}\varprojlim_{{\mathfrak{J}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}}{\mathfrak{R}}/{\mathfrak{J}}[Y]\\
\textstyle\simeq\prod_{X}{\mathfrak{R}}[[Y]]\simeq\prod_{X}\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}\big({\mathfrak{R}},{\mathfrak{R}}[[Y]]\big)\simeq\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}\big({\mathfrak{R}}[[X]],{\mathfrak{R}}[[Y]]\big),

in order obtain an isomorphism of the categories they form.
One also has to check that these isomorphisms agree with
the compositions of morphisms in the two categories.

In other words, we can conclude that the additive categories of
projective left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules and projective pseudo-compact right

{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules are
anti-equivalent
(cf.
[
67
, the end
of Remark A.3]
).

One would like to generalize this equivalence from locally finite to
locally Noetherian abelian categories, i. e., abelian categories
satisfying the axiom Ab5 and admitting a set of generators consisting
of Noetherian objects, or equivalently, Ab5-categories where every
object is the union of its Noetherian subobjects and isomorphism
classes of Noetherian objects form
a set
[
42
, § II.4]
(cf.
[
56
]
).

Remark
.

A remark at the end of
[
42
, § IV.3]
suggests considering
topological rings
{\mathfrak{R}}
with a base of neighborhoods of zero formed
by (say, right) ideals
{\mathfrak{J}}
such that the quotient modules
{\mathfrak{R}}/{\mathfrak{J}}

are Artinian, and topological right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules with a base of
neighborhoods of zero formed by open
{\mathfrak{R}}
-submodules with Artinian
quotient modules.
Then the opposite abelian category
{\mathsf{E}}({\mathfrak{R}})
to such category of

{\mathfrak{R}}
-modules is locally Noetherian, and the object
{\mathcal{C}}
opposite to
the right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-module
{\mathfrak{R}}
is injective in it.

However, the direct summands of direct sums of copies of the object

{\mathcal{C}}
do
not
exhaust the class of injective objects in

{\mathsf{E}}({\mathfrak{R}})
, as one can see already in the example of the topological
ring
{\mathfrak{R}}={\mathbb{Z}}_{l}
with the Artinian discrete right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-module

\mathbb{Q}_{l}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}
, which admits no surjective continuous
morphisms from topological products of copies of the right

{\mathfrak{R}}
-module
{\mathfrak{R}}
.
Furthermore, given a locally Noetherian abelian category
{\mathsf{A}}
,
choosing an injective object
{\mathcal{E}}\in{\mathsf{A}}
such that all
the injectives in
{\mathsf{A}}
are direct summands of the direct sums
of copies of
{\mathcal{E}}
leads to a topological ring

{\mathfrak{R}}=\End_{\mathsf{A}}({\mathcal{E}})^{\mathrm{op}}
which does
not
satisfy the above
Artinianness condition in general.

Indeed, it suffices to take
{\mathsf{A}}={\mathsf{Ab}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}=\mathbb{R}/{\mathbb{Z}}
;
then the Noetherian object
{\mathbb{Z}}\in{\mathsf{A}}
is embeddable into
{\mathcal{E}}

by means of any irrational number in
\mathbb{R}/{\mathbb{Z}}
, hence
the discrete right
{\mathfrak{R}}
-module
\Hom_{\mathsf{Ab}}({\mathbb{Z}},\mathbb{R}/{\mathbb{Z}})=\mathbb{R}/{\mathbb{Z}}
is the quotient module of

{\mathfrak{R}}=\End_{\mathsf{Ab}}(\mathbb{R}/{\mathbb{Z}})^{\mathrm{op}}
by a certain open
right ideal, and it is
not
an Artinian module.

Nor
does the functor assigning the
{\mathfrak{R}}
-module

\Hom_{\mathsf{Ab}}({\mathbb{Z}},\mathbb{R}/{\mathbb{Z}})
to the abelian group
{\mathbb{Z}}
appear
anywhere close to being an anti-equivalence of abelian categories.
That is why we choose a different path below.

Let us start from an arbitrary locally Noetherian abelian category
{\mathsf{A}}
.
Recall that, being a Grothendieck abelian category (an Ab5-category
with a set of generators), any locally Noetherian category has
enough injectives
[
45
, N
o
1.10]
.

Theorem
.

For any locally Noetherian abelian category
{\mathsf{A}}
there exists
a unique abelian category
{\mathsf{B}}
with enough projectives such
that the full additive subcategories of injective objects in
{\mathsf{A}}

and projective objects in
{\mathsf{B}}
are (covariantly) equivalent,

{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathsf{inj}}\simeq{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}.

All the infinite direct sums and products exist in the abelian
categories
{\mathsf{A}}
and
{\mathsf{B}}
, and both the subcategories of injective
objects in
{\mathsf{A}}
and projective objects in
{\mathsf{B}}
are closed under
both the infinite direct sums and the infinite products.

Proof.

To prove uniqueness, we notice that an abelian category with enough
projectives is determined by its full additive subcategory of
projective objects.
Indeed, given an abelian category
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}
with enough projectives
and an abelian category
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}
, any additive functor
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{proj}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}
can be uniquely extended to a right exact functor

{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}
.
In particular, let
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}
be two abelian categories with
equivalent full subcategories of projectives
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{proj}}\simeq{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{proj}}
.
Then the embedding functor
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{proj}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}
extends uniquely
to a right exact functor
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}
, while the embedding
functor
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{proj}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}
extends uniquely to a right exact
functor
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}
.
The compositions
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}
are right exact functors isomorphic to
the identity functors on the full subcategories of projective
objects, so they are also naturally isomorphic to identity functors
on the whole abelian categories
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime\prime}
.

Now, given a locally Noetherian abelian category
{\mathsf{A}}
, choose
an injective object
{\mathcal{E}}\in{\mathsf{A}}
such that all the injectives in

{\mathsf{A}}
are directs summands of the direct sums of copies of
{\mathcal{E}}
.
E. g., one can take
{\mathcal{E}}
to be the direct sum of injective
envelopes of all the quotient objects of Noetherian generators
of
{\mathsf{A}}
.
Consider the topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}=\End_{\mathsf{A}}({\mathcal{E}})^{\mathrm{op}}
with a base
of neighborhoods of zero formed by the right ideals opposite to
the annihilators of Noetherian submodules
{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}

in
\End_{\mathsf{A}}({\mathcal{E}})
.
Set
{\mathsf{B}}={\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
to be the abelian category of left

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules.

To identify the full subcategory of direct sums of copies of
the object
{\mathcal{E}}
in
{\mathsf{A}}
with the full subcategory of free

{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules in
{\mathsf{B}}
, one computes the Hom groups

\textstyle\Hom_{\mathsf{A}}\big(\bigoplus_{X}{\mathcal{E}},\medspace\bigoplus_{Y}{\mathcal{E}}\big)\simeq\prod_{X}\Hom_{\mathsf{A}}\big({\mathcal{E}},\medspace\bigoplus_{Y}{\mathcal{E}}\big)\simeq\prod_{X}\varprojlim_{{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}}\Hom_{\mathsf{A}}\big({\mathcal{L}},\medspace\bigoplus_{Y}{\mathcal{E}}\big)\\
\textstyle\simeq\prod_{X}\varprojlim_{{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}}\bigoplus_{Y}\Hom_{\mathsf{A}}({\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{E}})\simeq\prod_{X}\varprojlim_{{\mathfrak{J}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}}\bigoplus_{Y}{\mathfrak{R}}/{\mathfrak{J}}\\
\textstyle\simeq\prod_{X}{\mathfrak{R}}[[Y]]\simeq\prod_{X}\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}\big({\mathfrak{R}},{\mathfrak{R}}[[Y]]\big)\simeq\Hom^{\mathfrak{R}}\big({\mathfrak{R}}[[X]],{\mathfrak{R}}[[Y]]\big)

in both subcategories in terms of projective limits over
the Noetherian subobjects
{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
and the open right
ideals
{\mathfrak{J}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
.
Once again, one has to check that these isomorphisms agree with
the compositions of morphisms in the two categories.
Adjoining the direct summands (the images of idempotent endomorphisms)
to both the full subcategories, one obtains the desired equivalence
between the full subcategories of injective objects in
{\mathsf{A}}
and
projective objects in
{\mathsf{B}}
.

Any abelian category with infinite direct sums and a set of generators
has infinite products by Freyd’s Special Adjoint Functor existence
Theorem
[
57
, Corollary V.8]
; and the category of left contramodules
over a topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
with a base of neighborhoods of zero
consisting of open right ideals always has both the infinite direct
sums and products, as we have seen in Section
2.1
.
In any abelian category the infinite direct sums of projective
objects are projective and the infinite products of injective objects
are injective.

The infinite direct sums of injective objects in a locally Noetherian
abelian category
{\mathsf{A}}
are injective
[
42
, Corollaire II.4.1 and
Proposition IV.2.6]
.
Finally, to prove that the infinite products of projective objects in
our category
{\mathsf{B}}
are projective, we notice that any
family of objects of the full subcategory
{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}\subset{\mathsf{B}}

has an infinite product in
{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
(since any family of
objects of
{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathsf{inj}}
has an infinite product in
{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathsf{inj}}
).
It is claimed that whenever an abelian category
{\mathsf{B}}
has enough
projectives and the full subcategory of projectives
{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}\subset{\mathsf{B}}

has infinite products, these are also the infinite products of
objects of
{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
in the whole category
{\mathsf{B}}
, i. e., the embedding
functor
{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{B}}
preserves infinite products.

Indeed, let an object
X\in{\mathsf{B}}
be presented as the cokernel of
a morphism of projective objects
Q\longrightarrow P
, so that there is
an initial fragment of projective resolution
Q\longrightarrow P\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow 0
in
{\mathsf{B}}
.
Let
F_{\alpha}
be a family of objects in
{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
and

F=\prod^{{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}}_{\alpha}F_{\alpha}
be their product in
{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
.
Then one computes the group
\Hom_{\mathsf{B}}(X,F)
as the kernel of
the map of abelian groups
\Hom_{\mathsf{B}}(P,F)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathsf{B}}(Q,F)
, which
is isomorphic to the product of the kernels of the maps

\Hom_{\mathsf{B}}(P,F_{\alpha})\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathsf{B}}(Q,F_{\alpha})
, that is the group

\prod_{\alpha}\Hom_{\mathsf{B}}(X,F_{\alpha})
.
∎

A further discussion of the correspondence between the abelian
categories
{\mathsf{A}}
and
{\mathsf{B}}
described in the theorem can be found
in
[
91
, Examples 6.3–6.5]
and
[
90
, Section 10.2]
.

3.7.
\Add(M)
and projective contramodules

The following results and constructions from
the papers
[
90
,
91
,
79
]
provide a series of far-reaching
generalizations of the theorem and proof from the previous section.

Let
{\mathsf{A}}
be an additive category with infinite direct sums
and
M\in{\mathsf{A}}
be an object.
Then we denote by
\Add(M)\subset{\mathsf{A}}
the full subcategory in
{\mathsf{A}}

consisting of all the direct summands of (infinite) direct sums
of copies of the object
M
.

We start with the case of a module over an associative ring.

Proposition 1
.

Let
R
be an associative ring and
M
be a left
R
-module.
Then there is a complete and separated topological ring
{\mathfrak{S}}

with a base of neighborhoods of zero formed by open right ideals
such that the full subcategory
\Add(M)\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is equivalent
to the category of projective left
{\mathfrak{S}}
-contramodules,

\Add(M)\simeq{\mathfrak{S}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
.

Proof.

When the
R
-module
M
is finitely generated, the category

\Add(M)
is equivalent to the category of projective left

S
-modules,
\Add(M)\simeq S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
, for the discrete ring
S
of
endomorphisms of the
R
-module
M
.
This is a classical result (see
[
90
, Remark 7.2]
for
references and a further discussion).

To be more precise, in the general case
{\mathfrak{S}}=\Hom_{R}(M,M)^{\mathrm{op}}
is
the opposite ring to the ring of endomorphisms of the
R
-module
M
;
so the ring
{\mathfrak{S}}
acts in
M
on the right, making
M

an
R
-
{\mathfrak{S}}
-bimodule.
The topology on
{\mathfrak{S}}
is defined by the condition that
M
should be
a discrete right
{\mathfrak{S}}
-module; specifically, the annihilators of
finitely generated
R
-submodules
L\subset M
form a base of
neighborhoods of zero in
{\mathfrak{S}}
.
We refer to
[
90
, Theorem 7.1]
for the details.
∎

The next proposition provides a generalization to the categorical
context.

Proposition 2
.

Let
{\mathsf{A}}
be a locally finitely presentable, or more generally,
a locally finitely generated abelian category in the sense of
the book
[
1
, Sections 1.A and 1.E]
.
Let
M\in{\mathsf{A}}
be an object.
Then there is a complete and separated topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}

with a base of neighborhoods of zero formed by open right ideals
such that the full subcategory
\Add(M)\subset{\mathsf{A}}
is equivalent
to the category of projective left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodules,

\Add(M)\simeq{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
.

Proof.

As above, put
{\mathfrak{R}}=\Hom_{\mathsf{A}}(M,M)^{\mathrm{op}}
; so
{\mathfrak{R}}
is opposite ring to
the ring of endomorphisms of
M
, or in other words, the universal ring
acting in the object
M\in{\mathsf{A}}
on the right.
The annihilators of finitely generated subobjects
L\subset M
form
a base of the topology on
{\mathfrak{R}}
.
The proof is the same as in Proposition 1.
∎

Even more generally, let
{\mathsf{A}}
be a
locally weakly finitely
generated
abelian category with a generator, in the sense of
the paper
[
90
, Section 9.2]
or which is the same,
a
nearly locally finitely presentable
abelian category in
the sense of the paper
[
87
]
.
Then, for any object
M\in{\mathsf{A}}
, the full subcategory

\Add(M)\subset{\mathsf{A}}
is equivalent to the category of projective
left contramodules
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
over the topological ring

{\mathfrak{R}}=\Hom_{\mathsf{A}}(M,M)^{\mathrm{op}}
with a base of the topology formed by
the annihilators of
weakly finitely generated

(
=
nearly finitely presentable) subobjects
L\subset M
.
This is the result of
[
90
, Theorem 9.9]
.

A further generalization to
additive categories with closed
functors
discussed in
[
90
, Section 9.3]
makes the above
assertions applicable to the abelian categories
{\mathsf{A}}={\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}
and

{\mathsf{A}}={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--simod}}}
of comodules over corings and semimodules over
semialgebras
[
90
, Section 10.3]
.
These results allow to interpret the abelian categories of
left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules and left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodules

{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}
as the categories of contramodules
over appropriately constructed topological rings
{\mathfrak{R}}
, as mentioned
above in Sections
2.5
and
2.6
.

The following proposition is even more general.

Proposition 3
.

Let
{\mathsf{A}}
be an idempotent-complete additive category with infinite
direct sums, and let
M\in{\mathsf{A}}
be an object.
Then there exists a unique abelian category
{\mathsf{B}}
with enough
projective objects such that the full subcategory
\Add(M)\subset{\mathsf{A}}

is equivalent to the full subcategory of projective objects in
{\mathsf{B}}
,
that is
\Add(M)\simeq{\mathsf{B}}_{\mathsf{proj}}
.

Proof.

The uniqueness was explained in the proof of
Theorem
3.6
.
Concerning existence, there are two constructions of the category

{\mathsf{B}}
suggested in
[
91
, proof of Theorem 1.1(a)]
.
Fistly, one can construct
{\mathsf{B}}
as the category of

finitely presented
(
coherent
)
functors

\Add(M)^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Ab}}
.
Since the category
\Add(M)
has weak kernels, the category of
coherent functors on it is abelian.

Secondly,
{\mathsf{B}}
is the category of algebras/modules over the additive
monad
X\longmapsto\Hom_{\mathsf{A}}(M,M^{(X)})
on the category of
sets (see also
[
90
, Section 6]
and
[
79
, Section 1]
).
The advantage of this point of view is that it specializes to
the above descriptions of
{\mathsf{B}}
as the abelian category of contramodules
over a topological ring, under appropriate assumptions.
∎

An approach to the
Enochs conjecture on covers and direct limits

based on the results described above in this section was suggested in
the papers
[
80
,
9
,
10
]
.
An application of these results to direct limits of classes of modules
was developed in
[
93
]
.

3.8.
Fully faithful contramodule forgetful functors

Let
{\mathfrak{R}}
be a complete and separated topological ring with a base of
neighborhoods of zero formed by open right ideals.
Let
R
be an associative ring, and let
\rho\colon R\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}
be a ring
homomorphism.
Consider the composition of the natural forgetful functor

{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
with the functor of restriction of
scalars
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
.
When is the forgetful functor
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
fully faithful?

We have seen in Section
1.6
that the forgetful functors

k[[z]]{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow k[z]{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
are
fully faithful.
Moreover, for a right Noetherian associative ring
R
and its
completion
{\mathfrak{R}}=\varprojlim_{n}R/I^{n}
in the adic topology of
a centrally generated ideal
I\subset R
,
Theorem
2.2
stated that the forgetful functor

{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is fully faithful and explicitly described
its essential image.
Far-reaching generalizations of these relatively basic observations
were obtained in the papers
[
78
, Theorem 2.1]
,

[
79
, Section 3]
, and
[
81
, Section 6]
.

Let us start with the case of a coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}

[
78
, Section 2.2]
.
A coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
over a field
k
is said to be
coaugmented

if it is endowed with a coalgebra morphism (coagmentation)

\gamma\colon k\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
.
Over a coaugmented coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
, the one-dimensional vector
space
k
(as well as any other
k
-vector space) carries
the so-called
trivial
left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule structure, which is
defined in terms of the coagmentation.
The
cohomology
of a coaugmented coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
is
the graded vector space (or the graded algebra) of Yoneda Ext in
the category of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodules,
H^{*}({\mathcal{C}})=\Ext_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(k,k)
.
In particular, one has
H^{0}({\mathcal{C}})=k
, and the space
H^{1}({\mathcal{C}})=\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(k,k)
can be computed as the kernel of the comultiplication
map
\mu\colon{\mathcal{C}}_{+}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{+}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}_{+}
, where
{\mathcal{C}}_{+}={\mathcal{C}}/\gamma(k)
.

A coaugmented coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
is said to be
conilpotent

(see, e. g.,
[
68
, Sections 5.5 and 6.4]
) if for every element

c\in{\mathcal{C}}
there exists an integer
m\geqslant 0
such that the element
c
is annihilated by the composition
{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes m+1}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{+}^{\otimes m+1}
of the iterated comultiplication map

\mu^{(m)}\colon{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes m+1}
with the natural surjection

{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes m+1}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{+}^{\otimes m+1}
.
The vector space
H^{1}({\mathcal{C}})=\ker({\mathcal{C}}_{+}\to C_{+}^{\otimes 2})
is interpreted as
the vector space of
cogenerators
of a conilpotent coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}

(see
[
74
, Section 5]
for a discussion of cogenerators and
corelations of conilpotent coalgebras).
A conilpotent coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
is said to be
finitely cogenerated

if the
k
-vector space
H^{1}({\mathcal{C}})
is finite-dimensional.

Following the discussion in Section
2.3
, for any
coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
over a field
k
, a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule is the same
thing as a left contramodule over the pro-finite-dimensional
topological algebra
{\mathfrak{R}}={\mathcal{C}}^{*}
.
Hence we have a natural forgetful functor
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}

[
67
, Section A.1.2]
,
[
78
, Section 2.1]
.
Given a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
, the left action of
the
k
-algebra
{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
in
{\mathfrak{P}}
is constructed as the composition

{\mathcal{C}}^{*}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
of the natural embedding

{\mathcal{C}}^{*}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})
with the
{\mathcal{C}}
-contraaction map

\pi_{\mathfrak{P}}\colon\Hom_{k}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathfrak{P}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
.
As above, given an associative ring homomorphism
\rho\colon R\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
,
we obtain a forgetful functor
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
.

Theorem 1
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a finitely cogenerated conilpotent coalgebra over
k
.
Then the forgetful functor
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is fully
faithful.
Moreover, for any dense subring
R\subset{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
in
the pro-finite-dimensional topology of the
k
-algebra
{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
,
the forgetful functor
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

This is
[
78
, Theorem 2.1]
.
The argument is based on the contramodule Nakayama lemma for
coalgebras over fields
[
67
, Lemma A.2.1]
.
∎

Example 1
.

The following example is a particular case
of
[
79
, Examples 3.3]
.
Let
{\mathfrak{R}}=k\{\{z_{1},\dotsc,z_{m}\}\}
be the algebra of noncommutative
formal Taylor power series in a finite set of variables
z_{1}
, …,

z_{m}
over a field
k
.
We consider
{\mathfrak{R}}
as a topological ring in the formal power series
topology (or, in other words, the adic topology for the ideal
generated by the variables).
Then
{\mathfrak{R}}\simeq{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
is the dual pro-finite-dimensional algebra to
the
cofree conilpotent coalgebra

{\mathcal{C}}
with
m
cogenerators

z_{1}^{*}
, …,
z_{m}^{*}
.
Let
R=k\{z_{1},\dotsc,z_{m}\}
be the
k
-algebra of noncommutative
polynomials in
z_{1}
, …,
z_{m}
, and let
\rho\colon R\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}
be
the natural embedding.
According to Theorem 1, the forgetful functor
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is fully faithful.

Now we turn to a discussion of topological rings
{\mathfrak{R}}
with
a
countable
base of neighborhoods of zero.
Let
{\mathfrak{R}}
be a topological ring and
{\mathfrak{J}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
be a right ideal.
One says that a finite set of elements
s_{1}
, …,
s_{m}\in{\mathfrak{J}}

strongly generates

{\mathfrak{J}}
if for every family of elements

r_{x}\in{\mathfrak{J}}
, indexed by a set
X
and converging to zero in
the topology of
{\mathfrak{R}}
, there exist
m
families of elements

t_{j,x}\in{\mathfrak{R}}
,
j=1
, …,
m
, each of them indexed by
the set
X
and converging to zero in the topology of
{\mathfrak{R}}
, such that

r_{x}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}s_{j}t_{j,x}
for every
x\in X
.
Since any element
r\in{\mathfrak{J}}
can be viewed as a family of elements
indexed by a one-point set
X
, and any finite family of elements in

{\mathfrak{R}}
converges to zero in
{\mathfrak{R}}
by definition, any finite family of
elements
s_{j}
strongly generating a right ideal
{\mathfrak{J}}
also generates

{\mathfrak{J}}
in the conventional sense
[
79
, Section 3]
.

The following proposition is a generalization of Theorem 1 to
topological rings of much more general nature than
the pro-finite-dimensional algebras over fields.

Proposition
.

Let
{\mathfrak{R}}
be a complete and separated topological associative ring
and
R\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
be a dense subring.
Assume that
{\mathfrak{R}}
has a countable base of neighborhoods of zero
consisting of open two-sided ideals
{\mathfrak{J}}
, each of which, viewed as
a right ideal, is strongly generated by a finite set of elements
belonging to
R\cap{\mathfrak{J}}
.
Then the forgetful functor
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is fully
faithful.

Proof.

This is
[
79
, Theorem 3.1]
.
The argument is based on a suitable version of the contramodule
Nakayama lemma for topological rings (
[
70
, Lemma D.1.2]

or
[
86
, Lemma 6.14]
; see Lemma 1 below).
∎

An even more general result can be found in
[
81
, Section 6]
.
Let
{\mathfrak{R}}
be a complete and separated topological ring with a base
of neighborhoods of zero formed by open right ideals, and let

\rho\colon R\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}
be an associative ring homomorphism with a dense
image.
Then the full preimages of open right ideals in
{\mathfrak{R}}
under
\rho

form a base of a topology on
R
, making
R
a topological ring.
The ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
is the completion of the ring
R
in this topology.
Furthermore, the assignment
{\mathfrak{I}}\longmapsto I=\rho^{-1}(I)
defines
a bijection between open right ideals
{\mathfrak{I}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
and open right
ideals
I\subset R

[
81
, Section 4]
.

Generalizing the previous definition, we say that an open right
ideal
I\subset R
is
strongly generated
(or, in other words,
the corresponding open right ideal
{\mathfrak{I}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
is
strongly
generated by elements coming from
R
) if, for any set
X
and any

X
-indexed family of elements
r_{x}\in{\mathfrak{I}}
converging to zero in
the topology of
{\mathfrak{R}}
, there exists a finite set of elements

s_{1}
, …,
s_{m}\in I
and
m
families of elements

t_{j,x}\in{\mathfrak{R}}
,
j=1
, …,
m
, each of them indexed by
the set
X
and converging to zero in the topology of
{\mathfrak{R}}
,
such that
r_{x}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\rho(s_{j})t_{j,x}
for every
x\in X
.
When the topological ring
{\mathfrak{R}}
has a countable base of neighborhoods
of zero, it suffices to check this condition for families of elements

r_{x}
belonging to the dense subring
\rho(R)\subset{\mathfrak{R}}

[
81
, Lemma 6.4]
.

Moreover, as explained in
[
81
, Section 6]
, the property of
an open right ideal
{\mathfrak{I}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
to be strongly generated by elements
coming from
R
can be expressed consizely by the equation

{\mathfrak{I}}[[X]]=I{\mathfrak{R}}[[X]]
.
Here
{\mathfrak{I}}[[X]]\subset{\mathfrak{R}}[[X]]
is the subgroup of all
X
-indexed
zero-convergent families of elements from
{\mathfrak{I}}
in the left

{\mathfrak{R}}
-module of all
X
-indexed zero-convergent families of
elements from
{\mathfrak{R}}
.
According to
[
81
, Remark 6.5]
, an open right ideal
I\subset R

is strongly generated by a finite set of its elements (in the sense of
our previous definition) whenever it is strongly generated
and

is finitely generated as a right ideal in an abstract associative
ring
R
.

Theorem 2
.

Let
{\mathfrak{R}}
be a complete and separated topological ring with
a countable base of neighborhoods of zero consisting of open right
ideals, and let
\rho\colon R\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{R}}
be a ring homomorphism with
a dense image.
Then the forgetful functor
{\mathfrak{R}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is fully
faithful
if and only if
all the open right ideals
{\mathfrak{I}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}

are strongly generated by elements coming from
R
.
It suffices to check the latter condition for any chosen base of
neighborhoods of zero in
{\mathfrak{R}}
consisting of open right ideals.

Proof.

This is
[
81
, Theorem 6.2]
.
Once again, the proof of the “if” part is based on the contramodule
Nakayama lemma, which is formulated immediately below.
∎

Lemma 1
.

Let
{\mathfrak{R}}
be a complete and separated topological ring with
a countable base of neighborhoods of zero consisting of open right
ideals, and let
{\mathfrak{P}}
be a left
{\mathfrak{R}}
-contramodule.
Assume that one has
{\mathfrak{I}}{\rightthreetimes}{\mathfrak{P}}={\mathfrak{P}}
for every open right ideal

{\mathfrak{I}}\subset{\mathfrak{R}}
(see Section
3.3
for the notation).
Then
{\mathfrak{P}}=0
.

Proof.

This is
[
86
, Lemma 6.14]
(cf. Lemma
2.1
).
∎

In the rest of this section we discuss the full-and-faithfulness of
certain forgetful functors originating from the categories of
semicontramodules
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}
over semialgebras
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
over finitely
cogenerated conilpotent coalgebras
{\mathcal{C}}
(see
Section
2.6
).

Let us briefly recall a construction of semialgebras
from
[
67
, Section 10.2]
that was already used in
Sections
2.7
–
2.8
above.
Let
k
be a field,
{\mathcal{C}}
be a coalgebra over
k
, and
K
be
an associative algebra over
k
.
Let
K\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
be a homomorphism of
k
-algebras whose image
is dense in the pro-finite-dimensional topology of
{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
.
Then the related pairing
\phi\colon{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}K\longrightarrow k
is nondegenerate
in
{\mathcal{C}}
.
Following
[
67
, Section 10.1.4]
, the composition
{\operatorname{\mathsf{comod--}}}{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathsf{mod--}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{*}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathsf{mod--}}}K
of the natural functor
{\operatorname{\mathsf{comod--}}}{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathsf{mod--}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
(see Section
1.4
) with the functor of
restriction of scalars
{\operatorname{\mathsf{mod--}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{*}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathsf{mod--}}}K
is fully faithful.
The same applies to the similar composition of functors
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--comod}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}\longrightarrow K{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
between the categories of
left (co)modules.

Let
R
be another associative algebra over
k
and
f\colon K\longrightarrow R

be a
k
-algebra homomorphism such that
R
is a flat left

K
-module in the (bi)module structure induced by
f
.
Set
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}={\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{K}R
, where the right
K
-module structure on
{\mathcal{C}}

is provided by the above functor
{\operatorname{\mathsf{comod--}}}{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathsf{mod--}}}K
.
Then
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
is naturally an injective left
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule and
a right
R
-module.
Assume that the underlying right
K
-module structure on
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}

originates from a right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule structure (i. e., the right

K
-module
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
belongs to the essential image of the fully
faithful functor
{\operatorname{\mathsf{comod--}}}{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathsf{mod--}}}K
).
Then the
{\mathcal{C}}
-
{\mathcal{C}}
-bicomodule
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
has a natural structure of
a semialgebra over
{\mathcal{C}}
with the semiunit map
{\mathbf{e}}\colon{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
induced
by the morphism
f\colon K\longrightarrow R
and the semimultiplication map

{\mathbf{m}}\colon{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbin{\text{\smaller$\square$}}_{\mathcal{C}}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}\longrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
induced by the multiplication map

R\otimes_{K}R\longrightarrow R

[
67
, Section 10.2.1]
.

Lemma 2
.

Assume that
R
is a projective left
K
-module.
Then the abelian category of left
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
-semicontramodules

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}
is isomorphic to the category of
k
-vector spaces
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}

endowed with the structures of a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule and a left

R
-module satisfying the following two compatibility conditions:
firstly, the two underlying left
K
-module structures should coincide,
and secondly, the
R
-action map
{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}}\longrightarrow\Hom_{K}(R,{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}})
should be
a morphism of left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodules.

Here the left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule structure on the
k
-vector space

\Hom_{K}(R,{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{P}}})
is provided by the natural isomorphism

\Hom_{K}(R,{\mathfrak{P}})\simeq\Cohom_{\mathcal{C}}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}},{\mathfrak{P}})
, which holds for any left

{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule
{\mathfrak{P}}
.

In particular, there is a natural exact, faithful forgetful functor

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
forming a commutative square diagram with
the forgetful functors
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow K{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}

and
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}\longrightarrow K{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
.

Proof.

This is explained in
[
67
, Section 10.2.2]
.
∎

Corollary
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a coalgebra over a field
k
, let
K\longrightarrow R
be
a morphism of associative algebras over
k
making
R
a projective left

K
-module, and let
K\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
be a morphism of algebras with
a dense image.
Assume that the right
K
-module structure on
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}={\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{K}R
comes
from a right
{\mathcal{C}}
-comodule structure, so
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}
is a semialgebra
over
{\mathcal{C}}
; and assume further that
{\mathcal{C}}
is a finitely cogenerated
conilpotent coalgebra.
Then the forgetful functor
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is fully
faithful, and its essential image consists of all the left

R
-modules whose underlying left
K
-module structure comes from
a left
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule structure.

Proof.

By Theorem 1, the forgetful functor
{\mathcal{C}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--contra}}}\longrightarrow K{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is
fully faithful.
Hence the first assertion of the corollary follows immediately from
the first assertion of Lemma 2.
To deduce the second assertion of the corollary, it remains to observe
that, for any left
R
-module
P
, the action map
P\longrightarrow\Hom_{k}(R,P)

is a left
R
-module morphism, hence also a left
K
-module morphism,
and use Theorem 1 again.
∎

Example 2
.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
be a Tate (locally linearly compact) Lie algebra
(see Section
2.4
), and let
{\mathfrak{h}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
be a compact open
subalgebra.
Assume that the topological Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{h}}
is pro-nilpotent and
the discrete
{\mathfrak{h}}
-module
{\mathfrak{g}}/{\mathfrak{h}}
is nilpotent.
Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be the coassociative coalgebra related to
{\mathfrak{h}}
and

({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
be the Tate Harish-Chandra pair related to
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
{\mathfrak{h}}

(see Section
2.8
and
[
67
, Section D.6]
).
Assume that the coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
is finitely cogenerated, or
equivalently, the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{h}}
is topologically finitely generated
(see
[
67
, Section D.6.1]
for the isomorphism of cohomology
of the Lie coalgebra
{\mathcal{L}}={\mathfrak{h}}^{\vee}
and the coassociative coalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
).

Let
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
be a dense Lie subalgebra; then
\bar{\mathfrak{h}}={\mathfrak{h}}\cap\bar{\mathfrak{g}}
is a dense Lie subalgebra in
{\mathfrak{h}}
.
Consider the two enveloping algebras
K=U(\bar{\mathfrak{h}})
and
R=U(\bar{\mathfrak{g}})
.
Then the associative algebra morphism
K\longrightarrow R
induced by
the embedding
\bar{\mathfrak{h}}\longrightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{g}}
and the associative algebra
morphism
K\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
obtained as the composition

U(\bar{\mathfrak{h}})\longrightarrow U({\mathfrak{h}})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
satisfy the assumptions of
Corollary.
The semialgebra
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}={\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{K}R
is naturally isomorphic to
the semialgebra
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}={\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{U({\mathfrak{h}})}U({\mathfrak{g}})
from
Section
2.8
.

Hence the following description of the category of (Tate)
Harish-Chandra contramodules
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}}^{r}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--sicntr}}}
is
provided by Corollary.
The category of Harish-Chandra contramodules
{\mathfrak{P}}
over
({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})
is
isomorphic to the category of
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules whose underlying

\bar{\mathfrak{h}}
-module structure comes from a
{\mathcal{C}}
-contramodule structure.
In particular, the forgetful functor
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{g}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is fully faithful.

For example, let
{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
be the Virasoro Lie algebra,

{\mathfrak{h}}\subset\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
be a compact open subalgebra contained in
the topological span of the generators
L_{i}
,
i\geqslant 1
, and

\bar{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathrm{Vir}\subset\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir}
be a dense subalgebra.
Then
{\mathfrak{h}}
is a topologically finitely generated pro-nilpotent
Lie algebra, so the above considerations apply.
Thus the forgetful functor
{\mathsf{O}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbb{V}\mathrm{ir},{\mathcal{C}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Vir}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is fully faithful.
